- where the seat's budget can carry it: a session whose launch would need more than the smallest ceiling of the seat's or the company's capped windows — its context with the brief that continues it ([What a continued launch costs](#what-a-continued-launch-costs)) — is not continued, which the launch decides once that brief is written; a seat with no capped window continues a session of any size;
- and once per lost round: a round whose job is lost a second time starts a session of its own, rather than replay the state it was lost in toward the round's limit;
- and where its stamp states a save point: a capture an older version of the engine stamped states none, only the places it read the tool log at, which cannot say which calls came after it, so a relaunch by this version starts a session of its own instead, told what the turn's jobs did. A run parked on a person's question across that upgrade still continues in the box it parked in where that box is held when the answer comes, which needs no stamp; otherwise it starts fresh.

A session a job of the turn [ran out of context](conversation-sessions.md#a-session-that-ran-out-of-context-is-retired) in is continued by no relaunch, whichever copy it would be: its kept capture, the session the turn's first launch was handed, or the one its box holds. The relaunch starts a session of its own, and that job's collection takes no capture of the session that ran out.

A relaunch that continues no kept session of the turn's own continues the session its first launch was handed where the turn has kept none, or the session its box holds where it could before, as above — each only where the seat's budget can carry it with the brief that continues it ([What a continued launch costs](#what-a-continued-launch-costs)), the box's copy being the same session, as large or larger — and otherwise starts a session of its own, with the brief written for one. Each reason is logged as `executor_session_not_continued` — **never a failed launch**.

**Fetched and checked before it is carried.** The session is fetched sealed from the [object store](object-store.md) onto the launching node's disk and opened whole once before a byte of it reaches the box, so an object the store no longer holds, or one whose seal does not open, is found before the run's brief is chosen rather than part way through writing the archive into the box. A box that already holds the archive keeps its own copy, by the digest it keeps ([Claude Code](#a-claude-code-runs-session)); where the job before ran in this very box and its harness exited cleanly, nothing is fetched at all.

**A session its harness was cut off in** — every capture of a lost job, whatever packed it; every one the engine packed rather than the run's script: a job that died before its script could pack, or one still working that its box would not end; every one the script packed after a harness that did not exit cleanly, killed by a signal or its memory limit while its wrapper lived on; and every one the script packed on its way out of an end a stop, a loss or its turn's cap asked of its job (`job-ended`), its harness told to stop mid-step — **replaces the box's own copy**, which may end in what its harness never finished, and the run is told its session may end mid-call. **Its save point is not the calls the run's log had taken before the pack** either: a harness killed while a message streamed never wrote the calls it had made and had answered, one told to stop was measured recording the call it was cut off in only with that call's message already written, and a loss's read cannot say how its job went — so such a capture is placed as one of a job still working is ([Each launch's session, kept](#each-launchs-session-kept)), at the newest save point its launch is already known to hold, and a run that continues it is told every call of its round since then — and every note the turn has read, as notes it may already show, since such a session may lack its job's last take of the notes and is not known to hold its job's brief, which told the notes the session it continued lacked (below). Each harness takes such a session up as it stands, by its own rules: Claude Code's resume (2.1.310) records an error result for a tool call the previous process died in, saying its outcome is unknown, and then "No response requested.", and the script resumes it without `CLAUDE_CODE_RESUME_INTERRUPTED_TURN`, which would run the cut-off turn again before the brief, and cuts back to its last whole line the transcript and every subagent's thread under its sidecar directory ([A Claude Code run's session](#a-claude-code-runs-session)); OpenCode's export holds only the messages that settled (`session/transfer.ts` at 2.0.32 and 2.2.5), so a step that never settled is not in it.

**The copy a job's own box holds, where its harness was cut off.** A person's answer or a replaced sign-in that carries the round on in the job's own box without the turn's kept capture — none was kept, its keep was still under way when the relaunch's wait ran out, or was refused, or the company keeps no session — continues the copy of the session that box holds, which no capture describes. That copy holds every call the job made and every note it read only where the job's harness **ended its own run**: the job over, its harness gone cleanly by itself, its script packing it. So the read that collects a job **records on the job's own record how its harness ended** (`harness_end`: `own_run`, or `cut_off` by the same line a capture's save point is drawn by — a harness killed, crashed or told to stop, or gone with any status but 0; a job ended before its harness started or one its end could not stop; a box that packed no session), whether or not it takes or keeps a capture of the session, once its take is done and within the room the read leaves the take's own records, so the record never costs the take or the box's pause their time; once recorded cut off, a later read never records it whole, and nothing is recorded over a word this build does not know. A relaunch that carries the round on in the job's box reads it, and where it does not say `own_run` — the harness was cut off, or a build before this one collected the job and recorded nothing — the copy may lack calls its harness made and had answered without writing them: Claude Code 2.1.310 makes a call, and can have it answered, before it writes the call to its transcript. Nor is the copy known to hold the job's brief: nobody has measured whether 2.1.310 writes the message a continued session is handed before it answers a call (it was measured answering a fresh session's first two calls before that session's transcript file existed, but a fresh session cut off then leaves its box nothing to continue, and the relaunch starts a session of its own), and a job that ended before its harness started leaves the session it was handed. So the session there is told, after what carries it on:

- **that it was cut off** where the record says so, and **that it may have been** where it says nothing or says it in a word this build does not know: it may end in the middle of a call, and may not show the last of what its run did — said whether or not any call follows, since the call a harness was cut off in may be one of its own tools, which the tool log never holds, and in words that speak of no save;
- **every call of the round**, under the header of calls the session may not show (a call shown both there and in the session being one call, made once), with every call the round's tool log dropped from its middle counted: what a cut-off capture's continuation is told from its save point, from the round's start. Never only the job's own calls: a copy that lacks the job's brief lacks the calls that brief told — an earlier job's, or the call an earlier answer approved. Never the call a person approved for the answer that carries the round on, though: what carries it on says what that call returned, and a later attempt at the answer, which finds the call in the tool log, would otherwise tell it twice;
- **every note the turn has read**, under a header that counts them as notes the session may already show (a note shown both there and in the session being one note, sent once), in the order read and before the notes waiting for it, as a kept session cut off is told them: the job's last take of its notes is recorded read before its harness has them, and a copy that lacks the job's brief lacks the notes that brief told — the ones the session it continued lacked, an earlier job's among them — and no record says which those were.

Its skill guard holds the skills the job's launch armed, which the session held before the job began, and **none of the job's own loads**: one its harness never wrote is told as a call the copy may lack, and the skill is loaded again before a call it covers. A job whose harness ended its own run is told none of this — only what carries it on, every call the tool log took after the job that made it was replaced and every call nobody waited for by the time it returned, whose outcomes its copy is not known to hold ([below](#a-relaunch-continues-the-turns-session)), every note from the first read the record marks [unanswered](#notes-to-a-running-run) on, under the header of notes it may already show, since its copy lacks that note and holds the answered ones after it, and the notes waiting — and its guard holds its loads, but none either kind of call made: its copy holds every call the job made and every call the job's brief told, which is every other call of the round wherever that brief told the round's calls (a fresh run's brief tells none), though of a call its harness gave up on only that it timed out. Nothing of it is built where the job's box is gone, which no launch continues in. A read made while the run's ending is decided — a stop's, or a loss's ending — records nothing, since nothing carries that round on; a lost job whose round is carried on is read by a collection, which records it. **What this does not reach**: a copy that lacks the job's brief, and a kept capture cut off that lacks its own, lack the lead that brief opened with too — the turn's task, where the job continued the session its conversation's last turn left; a review that sent the round back; an earlier answer — which the engine keeps no copy of to tell again; and a fresh run that carries a round on is told none of the round's calls, as before, nor is the copy of it its box holds once its harness ends its own run, which is told only the calls the tool log took late.

**What that costs.** The told calls are held to the prior-work ledger's budgets as every continuation's are, and a rewrite of a long call by the seat's auxiliary model is charged before the relaunch, which is refused, launching nothing, where that rewrite fills the seat's window; the notes are told whole, as every brief tells them. The brief is the longer for every call of the round and every note the turn has read, for every copy not known to have ended its own run, which includes **every relaunch after a replaced sign-in whose refused harness exited with a status other than 0**, and every job a build before this one collected. Where a capture measured the copy's context — its job's newest, kept or not — the launch's floor ([What a continued launch costs](#what-a-continued-launch-costs)) counts that brief and grows with it: a launch offered the turn's kept capture beside the copy is held to the larger of their floors, since its box takes up the copy only where it cannot restore the capture, and where the copy's own floor is past what the seat's windows or its model's can carry, the launch does not continue the copy (`executor_session_not_continued`) — it continues the kept capture where its own floor fits, and otherwise starts **a session of its own**, which, as every fresh run that carries a round on after a loss, an answer or a replaced sign-in is, is told none of the round's calls: only that what the run before it did through the seat's tools did happen, and to check before doing any of it again. A copy no capture measured — every copy in a company that keeps no session — holds its launch to the floor alone whatever its brief, so that brief neither raises the floor nor starts the run fresh, and only the harness's own compaction bounds what it adds.

**Four briefs, and the box picks between two.** The run's brief is chosen by whether it continues the session and where:

| The run | Its brief |
|---|---|
| Continues the session in the box it was last worked in | The session's next message: what carries it on, then what it lacks (below) |
| Continues the session restored into another box | The same, then what a new box changes: a fresh checkout — for a run offered `code_checkout`, of a repository the box's setup clones, while one the session checked out of Gitlet is not in the box until it checks it out again, at its branch's head ([Repositories on Gitlet](#repositories-on-gitlet)) — the work-in-progress branch to check out, the uncommitted work gone, and that the session's absolute paths under the earlier box's home were that box's |
| Continues the session its conversation's last turn left, in a box of its own | Where this turn begins and what a new box changes, then the turn's own brief whole — its fresh brief, or a relaunch's reseed ([below](#a-conversations-next-turn-continues-its-last-turns-session)) |
| Starts a session of its own | The turn's ask, with the ledger of its earlier rounds, every note the turn has read and what became of the run before it — and, where a kept session it could read is not continued, that session and how to read it, never why it is not continued ([What a harness is told](#what-a-harness-is-told)) |

Whether a restore succeeds is finally the box's to find out — an archive that does not unpack, an import OpenCode refuses — so a run asked to continue a session carries **both** its continuation and the fresh brief, and its script hands its harness the one its restore came to: a new session never answers a brief written for a model that remembers. It carries both wirings of the seat's tools too, one per session its box may start, and installs the one of the session it started, which is how the box tells the bridge ([below](#which-session-the-box-started)). The launch publishes the brief it handed as the round's opening frame once it has its box, before the run starts — the started record, published before the launch knows its box, carries the fresh brief — and its `executor_collected` record says which brief it handed (`brief`), whether the run took the session up (`harness_session_continued`) and, where it did not, why (`harness_session_fell_back`).

**What a continued session is told.** Not its task again in full: the session holds the task as it read it where it holds the message that told it and, where its harness ended its own run, every call it made — one it gave up on with no outcome or a failed one, though the call ran on (below) — and every note it read; one cut off may not show all of them, and is told so and told them again (below). Its brief is what it lacks:

- **What carries it on.** A send-back: that its work was reviewed and sent back; the task restated, since a compacted session holds only its own summary of it; the rules its earlier rounds' calls are read by — what already ran and must not be issued again, what may be re-run, what to cite as a delivery — without their list, which the session holds; and what the review and the engine asked of the next round. A person's answer: the question and the answer, and, where the question was a call held for a person's approval, what became of the call — made, and what it returned, or, where what it returned is recorded but cannot be read on the node carrying the run on, made and whether the tool reported a failure — which tells the call they approved once: nothing below lists it. A loss: that it was cut off, and why. A replaced sign-in: that the sign-in was replaced.
- That it may end mid-call, where its harness was cut off when it was saved — or, for the copy its job's own box holds, where that job's harness is not known to have ended its own run: said to have been cut off where its record says so, and to perhaps have been where it says nothing.
- **Every call its run made through the seat's tools that the session may not have been answered when it was saved** — every call the tool log took after the read that is the session's save point, so after the point it was saved at and any still in flight then, whose outcome it may never have been handed, however much of a long round's middle the log dropped below it — with each call's outcome, as the [prior-work ledger](turn-engine.md#prior-work-ledger-across-self_iterate-rounds) renders a round's calls and within its budgets: an argument value or a failed call's error past its budget rewritten by the seat's auxiliary model, or left out by its size and digest where no rewrite can be had, and the reads past the ledger's cap counted rather than listed, under one cap for every list the brief tells, spent on the late calls' reads (below) first, since the session never shows their outcomes and they are few, so a continuation never lists more of a round's reads than the ledger's cap. They happened. Where its harness ended its own run the session does not hold them; **where it was cut off**, its save point is the newest its launch held rather than where it ends — on a fleet of this version alone the round's start, since a capture placed by its read is its launch's last — so the list can repeat calls the session holds: it is headed as calls the session may not show, never as calls it lacks, and a call shown both there and in the session is said to be one call, made once. That brief is the longer for it, and the launch's floor ([What a continued launch costs](#what-a-continued-launch-costs)) and any rewrite of a long call by the seat's auxiliary model grow with it. A round carried on in its job's own box, that copy continued rather than a kept capture, is told every call of the round where the job's harness is not known to have ended its own run (**The copy a job's own box holds**, above). Never the call a person approved for the answer that carries the session on, which what carries it on tells: a later attempt at that answer, its first attempt's relaunch refused after it made the call, finds the call in the tool log after the save point of every capture taken before it was made, and listed here as well it was told twice. A call an earlier answer of the round approved is listed as any call is, since nothing else in the brief tells it.
- **How many more calls after the save the tool log dropped from its middle**, which it keeps only as a tally (past two hundred calls a run's log keeps its first and last and tallies those between): an exact count, since the log numbers every call it takes — but for a call an older version of the engine appended in a fleet mid-upgrade, which carries no number, so where one is listed it may be counted here as well: an over-count, never an under-count — followed by the log's tally of every call it dropped, by tool, the line every other reader of a long run is given. The tally counts calls made before the save point as well, and a call counted only there has no outcome, so the run is told to check whether its tool already did what the call was for before doing it again.
- **Every call the tool log took only after the job that made it was replaced, and every call nobody waited for by the time it returned** ([below](#a-relaunch-continues-the-turns-session)), whatever the save point, apart from the calls above and under words of their own, whether or not its harness was cut off — and to the copy a job's own box holds whatever its job's harness's end, which where that harness ended its own run is told these calls alone, since it holds every other call of the round wherever its job's brief told the round's calls (above): such a call finished only after the run that made it stopped waiting for it — gave up on it, or was cut off or replaced — so the session may show it with no outcome, as still running, as interrupted or as failed, and every later continuation of the round tells it again, so it is said to be one call, made once, whose outcome as told here stands. A `submit_work` among them is told as no submission of the work, so the run makes its own. Each telling is in the brief, so in the launch's floor where a capture measured the session's context, and in any rewrite of a long call. Such a call a long round's log dropped from its middle keeps no mark and no number, so the log counts it, and every continuation is told that count under the same words whatever its save point, with the log's tally where no count of the calls dropped after the save carries it — never as a call the session lacks, which the session may show. One that came after the point is counted among the calls dropped after it as well, by its number: an over-count, never an under-count.
- **Every note a person sent the turn that the session has not read** — read after it was saved, by the run that went on in it or by a review, which is never the session's — and then the notes this brief is the first to read ([Where a note lands](turn-engine.md#where-a-note-lands)). **Where the session was cut off**, and for the copy a job's own box holds where that job's harness is not known to have ended its own run, the notes before those are **every note the turn has read**, in the order read, under a header that counts them as notes the session may already show, a note shown both there and in the session being one note, sent once: its job's last take of its notes is recorded read before its harness has them, and it is not known to hold its job's brief, which told the notes the session it continued lacked, an earlier job's among them, and no record says which those were. So a capture cut off states no note it holds, whatever its job read, and the brief that continues it is the longer for every note the turn has read, as a fresh brief is, which its launch's floor counts where its capture measured the session's context ([What a continued launch costs](#what-a-continued-launch-costs)). **Where its notes stop before a read the record marks unanswered** — a take whose write landed with its answer lost ([Notes to a running run](#notes-to-a-running-run)), whose note the session lacks though its harness ended its own run, while it holds the answered ones after it — the notes from that read on are told under the same header, and none before it: whichever job's take it was, and where the mark landed only after the capture was stamped, the relaunch reading the record's marks itself. A launch that tells such a note again records it read by the job it opens, at the marked take's own place among the reads, so a capture of that job counts it held; a mark that lands after a brief was built, which that brief did not tell as marked, stops the cursor of every capture after it. The copy a job's own box holds, its harness having ended its own run, is told them the same way. **And a note the run's bridge served a harness** — recorded served before the poll that handed it over was answered, and read only once the harness confirmed delivering it ([OpenCode's notes plugin](#opencodes-notes-plugin)) — may be in the session whatever the record says of its read, so every continuation tells it under the same header: read since by a review, among the notes it has not read, or still waiting, among the ones the brief is the first to read — where the header takes every note before the last one served, so the order read is kept, and only the notes after it follow plainly, each one the turn had not read before. A fresh run is told it as every note.

**A call that ends after its job's relaunch.** The bridge runs a call under the run's own context, never the request's, so a call can finish after its harness died, gave up on it or was replaced. Where a relaunch that carried the round on — a loss's, a person's answer's, a replaced sign-in's — has opened the run's tool log to the next job by then, the log still lists the call as one of the round — its entry, written before it ran, updated — numbered as any call is, and marks it `late`. So does that opening, for a call of the job it replaces that the log took after the read the relaunch's briefs were built from — while the relaunch was readied: its probe of the kept session, any rewrite of a long call by the seat's auxiliary model, the call a person approved — but never for the call a person approved, which the relaunch's lead tells. What a late call cost is charged to no job: the next job's resume would pay it as its own, and its own job is over. What it committed counts in the turn's outcome as any call's does (a message it posted, a work item it filed), whether the log still lists it or has dropped it from a long round's middle, since the log keeps the kinds of change the round's calls committed apart from the calls. No brief of the round had told it when the log took it, and the session of the job that made it may not hold it, so no save point holds a late call: every continuation of the round built after it landed is told it whatever its save point — a copy continued in its job's own box among them, whatever its harness's end — under words of its own (above), and again by every later one; no skill guard counts a skill it loaded, so the skill is loaded again before a call it covers; and the round's collection never takes a late `submit_work` for the round's submission, nor keeps it whole as it keeps the round's own: the job carrying the round on makes the round's submission, never told of the late one, and every continuation after it is told the late one as no submission of the work. A long round's log keeps a late call while it drops ordinary calls from its middle, and counts one it does drop. A call that ends after a relaunch that started a new round, as a send-back does, is not listed at all: the new round numbers its own calls. The runs answer serves the mark (`late`), and the Executors board tags such a call **finished after a relaunch**. **What this does not reach**: a job running as a late call lands — the one carrying the round on, or the one a relaunch was readying as it landed — is not told it while it runs; every continuation built after it lands is. And what a late call committed counts in the turn's outcome only where the log takes the call before its round's last collection reads the log, which waits for every call of the round still in flight up to the first of its own bound, the turn's cap and its process gone ([The tool bridge](#the-tool-bridge--a-seats-own-tools-from-inside-a-box)): one whose update lands later is in no outcome.

**A call its harness gave up on.** The bridge runs a call under the run's own context, so a call goes on after its harness stops waiting for it, which the bridge sees in three ways: the harness cancels the request (`notifications/cancelled`), as Claude Code and OpenCode do at their tool timeout, discarding a late answer, so its model reads the call as timed out; the harness hangs up on the call's response, which the bridge keeps going while the call runs ([The tool bridge](#the-tool-bridge--a-seats-own-tools-from-inside-a-box)), so the next progress finds it gone; or the job's session on the bridge ends with the call still running — the run settled, its next job's opening replaced it, the seat left the node, or a recovery opened the job again — and the end answers the call at once that it is still running ([The tool bridge](#the-tool-bridge--a-seats-own-tools-from-inside-a-box)), so its harness is handed that answer and never the call's own. Its effect happens all the same. A harness that gave up on a call can still end its own run, and its job park on a question with the call still running, so the call can land as one of its job's own before a capture of that session is placed by its read, or before a relaunch reads the round's calls as the job's own copy in its box holds them: counted there, it was told to no continuation, while the session held only that it timed out, and the run could make it again. So the bridge records on the call whether anybody still waited for its answer as its tool returned — the request cancelled, its response gone, or the job's session's end answered it first, which the bridge decides once, so no call is both handed its own answer and marked for that end — and the runs answer serves the mark (`abandoned`). No save point holds a call so marked: every continuation of the round built after it landed is told it whatever its save point, the job's own copy in its box whatever its harness's end, under the late calls' words (above), and again by every later one; no skill guard counts a skill it loaded, whose body never reached the session; and a long round's log keeps it as it keeps a late call, and counts one it does drop among them. What it committed counts in the turn's outcome as a late call's does, listed or dropped, and on the same condition: only where the log takes it before its round's last collection reads the log. Unlike a late call it is its job's own: what it cost is charged to that job, and a successful `submit_work` among such calls can be the round's submission, though a continuation told it is told, as of a late one, to make its own, which the collection replays over it. **What this does not reach**: a harness that stops waiting for a call without cancelling it, while its job's session on the bridge is still open as the call returns, where nothing the bridge writes finds its response gone before the tool returns: one that hangs up in the last 20 seconds of the call, after the bridge's last progress, or on a call whose request carries no progress token, on which nothing is kept going; and one that gives up while it still reads the response, which the bridge cannot tell from one still waiting. Such a call lands unmarked, and a save point holds it as any call, though its session holds it as timed out. Claude Code's own watchdogs on a call give up this way, answering its model with an error and cancelling nothing. On 2.1.310, at the settings the engine runs it with, its budget for a response's first byte and its check on a silent call no longer give up a running call: its answer starts at once and is kept going, and the box's settings hold each to the bridge's bound, at which Claude Code cancels ([Claude Code](#claude-code)). One check still can: every 30 seconds it gives up a call 90 seconds after its transport was marked lost unless a progress has cleared the mark since (by reading its bundle), and the mark is routine, made on every call in flight whenever Claude Code loses the session's standing GET stream, which it does 360 seconds after opening it (measured). A call whose response is gone is not among them: the bridge's next progress finds the response gone, and the call is marked. What that check gives up unmarked is a call whose progress a hop between the box and the node holds back for 90 seconds while the response stays intact and the bridge's writes succeed, and a call whose request carries no progress token, on which nothing clears the mark (measured: a 540-second call with no token, given up at 449.9 seconds, 90 after its session's GET stream was lost, and recorded as any call when its tool returned). And a harness that gives up on a call in the moment between its tool's answer and that answer's delivery, as the bridge records the call. And a call still running as its run is collected — its run settled or parked on its question, a person's stop, the turn's cap or a reclaim ending its job, each of which ends the session and waits for no call — is waited for by that collection on the run's tool log, on whichever node makes it, up to the first of its own bound, the turn's cap and its process gone ([The tool bridge](#the-tool-bridge--a-seats-own-tools-from-inside-a-box)); one still running past that is read as a call whose outcome is unknown, and what it committed is in no outcome unless its update lands while the run's record stands. One whose update lands once the run's ending has deleted the record is on no record of the run at all, though its effect happened: the ending's delete names it as it goes (`sandbox_run_deleted_with_calls_in_flight`), and the engine's log names it again at warning as it returns (`bridge_abandoned_call_after_its_job`). And a call still running when the process running it is killed never makes its update: its entry stays on the run's record, the call counted by its identity and its outcome unknown; a node's graceful stop waits for it once it has given its seats back ([Graceful shutdown](agent-runtime.md#graceful-shutdown)).

Every word in it that somebody else wrote is fenced or defused under the relaunching segment's token, and every marker of any token in it escaped, so nothing the session read in an earlier segment opens or closes a fence of this one ([ADR-0040](https://github.com/crewlet/crewlet/blob/main/adr/0040-content-a-model-reads-carries-its-provenance.md)).

**Its skills follow the session.** A continued session has the skill bodies it loaded in front of it, so a relaunch that continues it arms its [skill guard](tool-skills.md#session-scope--why-per-phase-not-per-turn) with exactly those — the skills the launch of the job it follows was armed with, and those that job's own session loaded, through the point its capture was saved at where the capture is what is restored (of a session cut off, the newest point its launch held, so a skill it loaded after that is loaded again before a call it covers), and all of them where the job's own copy in its box is continued, but only where its harness ended its own run — and never another session's: not an earlier round's, not an earlier job's of the round, and not one a late call loaded (above). One that starts a session of its own starts with an empty guard, and so does one that continues the session its conversation's last turn left: the skills that session loaded were another turn's, which its lead says were set up anew. The guard is armed when the launch has decided which session its run takes up, once it knows its box and before the run starts, so a launch that finds it cannot continue the session after all — a box whose harness cannot take it up, an object the store no longer holds, a session the seat's budget cannot carry — arms an empty one ([Runtime invariants](turn-engine.md#runtime-invariants) 7 and 8). A run whose box finds only once it starts that its restore failed — an archive that does not unpack, an import OpenCode refuses — starts a session of its own, **and its box says so before the run's first call**, so the run is held to that session's guard, an empty one ([Which session the box started](#which-session-the-box-started)).

### Which session the box started

Whether a restore succeeds is the box's to find out, as its run starts, and the skill guard has to be the session's the run is in before the bridge admits a single call. So a launch that continues a session hands its box the seat's tools under **two bridge tokens of the job**: one for the session it continues, and one for a session of its own its script starts in that one's place. Once its restore has come to whatever it came to, the run's script installs the one of the session it started — Claude Code's MCP configuration, OpenCode's configured values — and removes the other from the box, before its harness starts. So the token the harness dials is the box's word on which session it started, read by the [bridge](#the-tool-bridge--a-seats-own-tools-from-inside-a-box) where it reads every request's token:

- **On the run's first request, before any call.** The first request on either token — the harness's MCP handshake as often as not — holds the run's guard to the session the box started before it is served, and every request arriving meanwhile waits for that: the guard the launch armed for a continued session where the box took it up, an empty one where it started its own. In that case the node that launched the run states, as the round's opening frame, the fresh brief the run was handed in the continuation's place — published beside the request rather than on its path, so a broker's slow moment never holds the harness's handshake past its connect timeout — and so, on such a moment, possibly after a later frame of the round, its completion included, since nothing on the engine's path waits for it — and given up if the broker has not taken it within five seconds, which leaves the continuation as the round's opening frame; the run's collection still says it started a session of its own (`harness_session_continued` false, with `harness_session_fell_back`'s reason). A node that took the run over before its box first reached the bridge holds the run to that empty guard all the same and writes what the box said on the job's record, but holds no copy of the brief the launch wrote: there the round's opening frame stays the continuation, and the run's collection says it started a session of its own (`harness_session_continued` false, with `harness_session_fell_back`'s reason). A later request on the other token is refused: the box started one session, and holds that token no longer. A box whose harness never reaches its bridge makes no call, and no guard is asked.
- **Not something the run's model can forge.** The session is in what the fleet signs, and by the time the model can read a file the box holds only the token of the session it is in. The script's own record of its restore, which the collection reads into the run's `executor_collected` (`harness_session_fell_back`, which stays its record there), is a file in the box the model could rewrite, and is never what a guard is built from.
- **Kept on the job's record.** What the box said is written on the run's record, and every later rebuild of the run's guard reads it: the collection's, a relaunch that carries the run on after a person's answer, a loss or a replaced sign-in, a reviewer's send-back that continues the run's session, and a node taking the run over while it works. The skills the launch armed are the session's only where the box said it took that session up. And it names **the session the job ran**: every runner names its run's session before its harness starts — the one it continues, or one it mints — and, beside one it continues, the session its script starts of its own in that one's place. So where a person's answer or a replaced sign-in carries the round on in the box it parked or was refused in **without the turn's kept capture** — none kept, or none the relaunch's runner restores — it continues there whichever of the two the box said it started: never the one the launch chose where the box fell back, whose transcript never read the skills the job loaded, and never one a file in the box names, such as the session record the run's collection reads, which the run's model could rewrite. A launch that continued nothing ran the session it named. A row that says nothing — a box that has not reached its bridge, a record the coordination store did not take — reads as a session of the job's own for its guard: the safe direction, since a guard short of a skill asks the run to load it again, where one holding a skill its session never read would let through a call the skill exists to hold back. And for a launch that continued a session, such a row names neither of the two as the session its box ran, so no relaunch continues either of them by that word: an answer or a replaced sign-in carries the round on from the turn's kept capture, or in a session of its own. **A kept capture names its session as its take read it**: off the session record the run's script writes as its harness exits, naming the session it started, for which the archive is packed and which the executor log's row then names. A relaunch continuing that capture continues that session — restored into whichever box it gets, or, in the very box it was taken in after a clean exit, as that box's own copy, whatever the box said. The record is a file the run's model could rewrite, as it could the transcript the session is, so the name is the box's no less than the content is; what the box's word decides there is the guard, which such a relaunch arms with the job's own loads through the capture, and with the skills the job's launch armed only where the box said it took that session up.

### What a continued launch costs

A continued run sends its session's whole context again before it reads a word of its brief, and every request it makes carries that context — mostly served from the prompt cache, and every token of it counted against the seat's windows, the cached share included ([Cache share](../guides/budgets-and-spend.md#cache-share)). So:

- **Its launch's floor** is the larger of `turn_engine.executor_min_budget_tokens` and the session's context with the brief that continues it: the whole input of the session's last request, cache included, read off the session's own transcript as it was captured (Claude Code's last main-thread assistant message's usage; OpenCode's last settled assistant message's tokens), and the estimate of the longer of its two briefs, since which box the launch gets is the launch's to learn. That holds for the job before's own copy in its box as much as for its kept capture — its context is what the job's newest capture measured, kept or not — and a session no capture measured (keeping switched off) has no known context, its launch held to the floor alone. **Each session the launch may continue is held to its own floor**: a relaunch offered both the kept capture and the job's own copy in its box, each with a brief of its own, is held to the larger of their floors, since its box takes up the kept one where it can restore it and the copy only where it cannot; and one of them whose own floor no capped window, or no request under the model's window, can carry is not continued, while the launch still offers the other, `executor_session_not_continued` saying why. **The floor is the member's the launch picks**: the launch picks its member of the seat's chain once, as it launches — the first not sitting out its plan — before it reckons the floor, and a kept session written by another harness than that member's is started fresh, held to the floor alone; the briefs that continue it were written beforehand wherever some member of the chain could take it up, since a plan can be spent, or come back, between the two. The job before's own session in its box counts only where that member can continue in that box: the same harness in the same cell, and a box on the engine host only on the node that launched it. A relaunch refused below its floor is held — or, an answer's, turned back — on that figure; a turn's first launch that continues its conversation's session, whose context is the base's own as its row recorded it, is refused on its dispatch, and the dispatcher parks the seat's mail on that figure — never on the floor alone, which a window with room for a fresh launch but not for the session meets, and which handed the delivery straight back into the same refusal. Its reason says what the figure is made of ([Budgets and Spend](../guides/budgets-and-spend.md#budget-windows)).
- **Its context is bounded by its harness's compaction.** Every Claude Code run is pinned to **the context window of the model it works under** (`CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW`, set by the wrapper over whatever the seat, the image or a profile set, and from 2.1.310 by the run's own settings over a repository's, with the variables that would switch compaction off or move the point it compacts at unset, and the two that move it blank in those settings too), and Claude Code compacts its conversation at that window less its own headroom: a reserve for its reply of up to 20,000 tokens, then a 13,000-token buffer. So no request of a run, or of a run that continues its session, carries more than about its model's window. OpenCode compacts near its model's own window — once a conversation reaches the window less 15% of it, at least 16,000 tokens, on 2.2; less 10% on 2.0 — which the engine does not move: OpenCode's setting for it (`compaction.buffer`) is a share or a count of the model's window, which the engine does not know, and a count at or past the window has 2.0 compact on every request. So a continued OpenCode run's request carries up to about its model's window less that share. A session past the window — an OpenCode one, or a Claude Code one whose repository switched its compaction off — is continued wherever the seat's budget can carry it, its floor saying so. A Claude Code session is not continued on a member whose model's window is too small for it: where its context with the brief that continues it is more than one request under that window can carry, its run starts a session of its own, and `executor_session_not_continued` says why.

**The window is the model's own.** The engine reads it from the model argument the launch hands Claude Code (an alias such as `opus`, an id such as `claude-opus-5-5`, the same id with a dated or a platform suffix, or a `[1m]` opt-in): 1,000,000 tokens for every current Claude model, 200,000 for Claude Haiku 4.5, Opus 4.5 and Sonnet 4.5 (1,000,000 with `[1m]`). A model it does not know is pinned to **200,000**, the standard window of every Claude model, and the launch logs `executor_context_window_unknown` naming it; Claude Code lowers any pin to the model's own window, so a pin is never past what the model takes. A fixed window below the model's starves the run instead: its first request already carries the harness's own prompt and tools, the seat's bridged tools, its standing instructions and its brief, and under a 100,000-token window Claude Code compacts at 67,000, so in a large repository a few file reads refilled the context within three turns of each compaction and the harness stopped the run as thrashing (`rapid_refill_breaker`, recorded as `context_exhausted`), on a fresh session as much as a continued one. The cost of the larger window is per request: a continued Claude Code run of *n* requests on a 1,000,000-token model is charged up to about *n* × 967,000 input tokens, most of them cache reads, and an OpenCode run up to *n* times its model's compaction point, every token of it counted against the seat's windows and held to its launch's floor. The engine records the window each Claude Code run was pinned to on its collected record (`context_window_tokens`).

## A conversation's next turn continues its last turn's session

A turn that serves a conversation — the thread, the issue, the pull request or the work item its trigger names — **continues the session its conversation's last turn left**: its first launch restores the conversation's **head** into a box of its own, and its run takes that session up, told where the new turn begins. What the earlier turn did and said is in that session; what no transcript holds — how each earlier turn ended — the engine tells it.

**The head is the turn that read the conversation last.** Of the conversation's ended turns whose rows on the executor log still name a kept session, it is the one whose own first launch read the conversation latest — the newest end among turns that read it at one position, and a turn whose read failed after every turn that read — never simply the one that ended last. Every first launch continues the head its read named, so a conversation's sessions are a line, each continuing the one before, and a turn that read the conversation early can end after turns that read it later: one parked on a person's question frees its seat, and the conversation's next message, offered to it as the answer first, runs beside it only where it is not taken; a schedule's next fire, never an answer, runs beside a parked fire every time. Such a turn continued none of what those later turns left, so it is told and offered beside the head, never continued, and the next turn's end absorbs it. The seat's [session budget](conversation-sessions.md#configuration) still releases the oldest end first, so under budget pressure it can release a head before a stale sibling — a turn that read the conversation earlier and ended later — and the next turn then continues, of the sessions the conversation still keeps, the one that read it last, and is told the released head's ending as it is told every other.

**A schedule's fires are one conversation.** The scheduler stamps every fire with its schedule's conversation, so a schedule is a conversation of each seat that runs it and its next fire there is that conversation's next turn, read, frozen, held, continued and absorbed exactly as below, and told in the schedule's words: **How this schedule's earlier fires ended**, and a lead that says a new fire of the schedule begins and that the schedule firing again, not a message, woke it ([Scheduling](scheduling.md#each-fire-continues-the-last-fires-session)).

**Read once, at the turn's dispatch.** The turn reads its conversation once, as its dispatch starts it, through the [estate router](../guides/satellite-nodes.md) at `linearizable`, so an end another node published is in the copy that answers: the head, and every other ended turn of the conversation that no ended turn was shown (the newest sixteen, the rest counted). From that one read it renders how those turns ended and decides what its launches continue; no launch of the turn reads the conversation again, so a turn of the same conversation that ends meanwhile moves neither. A turn with no conversation key — a colleague's ask, a generic source's wake — reads nothing, and neither does a turn of a company with `turn_engine.conversation_session.enabled: false`. A read that fails, or that the node's copy refuses, is logged (`executor_conversation_unread`), and the turn runs with no account of its conversation and a session of its own: a turn with less context, never one that cannot work.

**How the conversation's earlier turns ended.** Every brief of the turn — the fresh brief, a relaunch's reseed, the brief a continued session is handed after its lead — carries a section of its own above `## Task`, **How this conversation's earlier turns ended**: for each turn, oldest first, how it ended (`completed`, `failed`, `stopped` or `lost`) and its reviewer's last word; whether its reply reached the party waiting and on what surface, or that whether it did is not known; what its reviewer said landed; what its executor said blocked it; who stopped it and why; what failed, as the engine recorded it; that its newest session was not kept, and why; the engine's answer to a colleague's ask it took up; and what it was asked. Its rules: a reply that reached the party waiting is not to be said again, one that did not may still be owed, everything else a turn did already took effect, and where the history and the task below disagree, the task wins. **It is bounded at 24 000 bytes** as the run's record carries it, escapes included, about six thousand tokens: the head always, then the other turns newest first, each whole, while they fit; the rest are counted (`N earlier turns of this conversation are not shown here`) and stay for the next turn, since a turn's end absorbs only the turns its brief told. Every account in it somebody else wrote — what a turn was asked, its reviewer's and its executor's words, a stop's reason, the engine's accounts that quote a box or a store — is fenced under the turn's token as `turn_ending`, at the least trust, and the name of whoever stopped a turn is defused in place ([ADR-0040](https://github.com/crewlet/crewlet/blob/main/adr/0040-content-a-model-reads-carries-its-provenance.md)). It is rendered once, at the dispatch, and frozen on every park, so every brief of the turn, on whatever node and in whatever segment, tells the same bytes.

**Frozen at the first launch, and held on the log.** The turn's first launch freezes the read on the run's record it creates — where it was read, the turns its brief told, and the head as the turn's **base** — and no write that opens a later launch changes it. It records the read on the executor log as the turn's `begin`, right after that record exists and before any box does, asked for at most fifteen seconds on the launching frame, and the turn's row then **holds the base** against every release but a retirement — a sibling turn's end, the seat's budget, the retention, though not the end of a turn that [ran out of context](conversation-sessions.md#a-session-that-ran-out-of-context-is-retired) in it — until the turn's first landed capture or its end ([ADR-0073](https://github.com/crewlet/crewlet/blob/main/adr/0073-a-seats-executor-session-is-a-sealed-object-its-turns-row-names.md)). A begin the log does not hold in time leaves the launch continuing no base; one that lands later holds no longer than the turn's first capture or its end. Every capture of the turn carries the read its first launch froze, so its end absorbs the turns it was handed — the base among them, continued or not — and nothing it was not told. And a turn that has kept no session of its own when its first job is lost — its box gone with its host, its capture refused or never answered — is [relaunched](#a-relaunch-continues-the-turns-session) into the same base, on whichever node holds the seat by then, never a head read again: otherwise one lost job would end the conversation's session line for good. Once the turn has kept a session of its own, that one holds the base and everything after it, and only the relaunch rule above applies.

**In a box of its own, told where the turn begins.** A box the turn's earlier job ran in holds that job's working tree and session, which are the turn's to continue in place, so the base is continued only in a box of the launch's own. The launch reads the base's row again, at `linearizable`, and takes it up only while it still names the session the read froze; then, as for a [kept session](#a-relaunch-continues-the-turns-session), the runner and the box's harness must take it up, and its object is fetched sealed and opened whole before a byte reaches the box. The run is handed a lead, then the turn's own brief whole — its fresh brief, or a relaunch's reseed:

- that everything above is the conversation as its turns left it, through the base's turn, which ended at its instant — or, where that turn's end said it **went on past** the session its row names, up to that session, kept while the turn still worked and at what instant, what the turn did after it not being above and how the turn ended told below, saying too where the turn was then lost; or, where the turn was lost and its end said nothing of going on — the engine, not the turn, ended it, or its end was never recorded — through it, the session perhaps not holding the last of what it did — and that a new message has woken it, the task below being this turn's;
- that what it read above may be stale, and that tools it activated and skills it loaded above were set up anew for this turn — its [skill guard](tool-skills.md#session-scope--why-per-phase-not-per-turn) starts empty, whatever the base's session loaded;
- that it is in a new box on a fresh checkout — for a run offered `code_checkout`, of a repository the box's setup clones, while one the session checked out of Gitlet is not in the box until it checks it out again, at its branch's head ([Repositories on Gitlet](#repositories-on-gitlet)) — what the session had not committed and pushed gone with the box it was worked in, and that absolute paths under the base's home were that box's;
- and, where the base's capture was cut off, that it may end mid-call.

The launch's record says `continued_conversation`; the run carries the fresh brief beside the lead, for its script to hand a session of its own where its restore fails in the box, and its box says which session it started ([below](#which-session-the-box-started)).

**What it costs, and where the seat waits.** A run that continues a conversation's session sends that session's whole context first, as a relaunch does: its launch is held to the base's context, as its row recorded it, with the lead and the brief beside it ([What a continued launch costs](#what-a-continued-launch-costs)); a base no capped window can ever carry is not continued; and a first launch refused below that figure **parks the seat's mail on it** until a window has room for it ([Budgets and Spend](../guides/budgets-and-spend.md#budget-windows)).

**What starts fresh.** A conversation's first turn, and one whose every earlier turn's session was released, absorbed or aged out — nothing was there to continue, which is not logged — and, each logged as `executor_session_not_continued`: a turn with no conversation key, the switch off, a read that failed; no member of the seat's chain that restores the base, or a launch on a member running another harness; a base no window can carry; a begin the log did not hold; a box whose harness cannot take the session up, an object the store no longer holds, a restore that fails in the box; and a launch that reuses its turn's earlier box.

**A turn of a conversation that ends in its own frame leaves its ending too.** One that never launched and ended without an error — refused at the delegation cap — and a first launch abandoned once its record was written record their end from the frame that dispatched them ([How a turn ends on the executor log](turn-engine.md#how-a-turn-ends-on-the-executor-log)), so the conversation's next turn is told how they ended, and an abandoned launch's hold lifts at once. A turn whose frame failed before it launched records none: its delivery is handed back, to be parked or run again.

**Siblings are told and absorbed, never continued.** A turn that ran beside the head and ended with a session of its own is told how it ended, and absorbed by the turn's end with the rest of what its brief told; no launch holds it or opens it.

## Setup steps — provisioning the box

A harness's box needs environment wiring beyond the harness itself: git auth, registry credentials, toolchains. Provisioning is a declarative **setup-step** framework (`internal/sandbox/setup.go`); each `sandbox.SetupStep` contributes:

- `files` — content written into the box (helper scripts, config files), at paths that are not shell-expanded but are cleaned, in every cell, so a trailing `/` or a `.` segment names the same file. A relative path is under the box's home, and so is one starting `~/`, which names the home in every cell; that is how to name a file a step needs (`.npmrc`, `~/.config/tool.toml`), since it lands in the home whichever cell the box runs in. A local box writes one only inside its home — a `direct` box's is minted per box, so no absolute path names it, and a `container` box's is its mount at `/home/user` — so a step that must put a file anywhere else has a command do it, with whatever rights that box's commands run with, and a document whose step writes a file a seat's box would refuse is **refused when it is written**, naming the step, the path and the seat (see [Local sandboxes](#local-sandboxes)). An `e2b` box writes a file at any other path — its in-box agent creates it where the path says, missing directories included, and gives it to the account every template runs as (`user`), whose home a relative path lands in — but refuses another account's home (`~name/…`) and a path naming a directory: the home itself and the directories above it, which every template has, are refused on the write too, while a directory only the template made (`/tmp`) is refused by the box at setup. A setup *command* there runs as that same account, not as root;
- `commands` — shell commands run after the files land; a non-zero exit **fails the acquisition** and tears the box down, so a half-provisioned box never receives a brief promising an environment it doesn't have, and its status says whether a wait clears the failure (75 for one that does, below). Each runs for at most its step's `timeout_seconds` (ten minutes unless set), and the steps run one after another — the harness's install first — however long they take together: the launch keeps the box alive throughout, refreshing its TTL every 15 s as the completion poll does a running box's, so only a command's own timeout ends a slow one;
- `env` — variables merged into the harness's run env (`${VAR}`-resolved with the rest);
- `optional_keys` — keys of the step's `env`, and paths of its `files`, whose value may resolve to nothing: such a variable is left out of the run and such a file is not written, quietly, where any other reference that resolves to nothing is left out the same way and the run's start logs it as a gap, `sandbox_env_unresolved` (see [Credentials and the run environment](#credentials-and-the-run-environment));
- `brief` — a paragraph folded into the harness's "## Your environment" block, one of its [standing instructions](#what-a-harness-is-told), *telling* it what the step made true (an agent that wastes rounds rediscovering how to authenticate is slower than one that knows).

Steps come **from company config** — for any other host the engine ships none of its own, git auth included — applied in order: `providers.sandbox.setup` (engine-wide, every agent seat's box) then `role.sandbox.setup` (the seat's own extras). The one exception is a repository on Gitlet, which a run is *granted* rather than told about: the commands that clone it are the engine's, answered by the `code_checkout` that grants it for the run to run itself, never applied as setup (see [Repositories on Gitlet](#repositories-on-gitlet)). Setup commands execute **with the launch's environment** — the run env, plus whatever the engine minted for this job — so a recipe can read the engine's per-launch identity facts (`$CREWLET_AGENT_HANDLE`, `$CREWLET_AGENT_EMAIL`) and its own configured tokens at provisioning time. The run's telemetry endpoint is the exception: it is minted as the job starts and reaches the harness alone, since its token's lifetime runs from its minting and no setup step reads it. A reused box skips re-apply — its provisioning survives with its disk state — except for a file holding the seat's GitHub App token, which its launch brings up to date before the job starts ([below](#the-git-auth-recipe)); a repository on Gitlet it does not hold yet is one `code_checkout` away, as on a fresh box.

**A command's status says who clears its failure.** A setup command that fails is a setup an administrator has to fix (`configuration`), whatever status it exits with, 124 included (its `timeout_seconds` ran out), but one: **75**, `EX_TEMPFAIL`, which `sysexits.h` defines as a temporary failure. A command that exits 75 says a download its setup makes failed for now, which a wait clears rather than a person (`setup_unavailable`, see [When a run does not start](#when-a-run-does-not-start)). Only the command knows which of its failures a wait clears, so it says so itself: retry a fetch where it fails, so a blip never fails the step, and exit 75 only when the service is still answering, once those retries are spent, that it is failing on its side (a 408, a 429 or a 5xx). Never put `|| exit 75` on a whole command. It says a wrong pin, a refused credential or a blocked network will clear by waiting, which none of them does, and inside a pipeline it binds to the command before it alone: `curl … || exit 75 | sha256sum -c -` never checks a download that succeeded. [Recipe 1](#bring-your-own-image) does both for npm and for `curl`. It matters more than a wait: a failure classed `configuration` twice in a row pauses its seat until a person resumes it or the setting changes ([Agent runtime § Pausing a seat](agent-runtime.md#pausing-a-seat)), so a download that fails for now and does not exit 75 pauses the seat.

Every step has a `name`, unique within its list: it is what a setup failure points at, and because a step's `env` and `files` are credentials, it is also what a config read masks them under and what [sending the read back restores them by](configuration.md#reads-and-export). A document with two steps of one name in the same list is refused on every write; the same name in `providers.sandbox.setup` and in a seat's own `sandbox.setup` is fine. A config read shows a step's `env` and `files` values only when each is exactly one `${VAR}` reference.

**A file is content, and it reaches the box as written.** A step's `env` is a setting: a `${VAR}` anywhere in it is the engine's, expanded at launch with the rest of the run environment. A file's body is written into the box, so a `${…}` inside it belongs to whatever reads the file there — the script's shell, npm reading `.npmrc` — and the engine never expands one. The single exception is a body that is **exactly one** `${VAR}` and nothing else: that names the whole file, and at launch the box receives the value the variable holds (the secret store first, then the engine's environment), byte for byte. So `//registry.npmjs.org/:_authToken=${NPM_TOKEN}` in an `.npmrc` is left for npm to expand from the `NPM_TOKEN` the step's `env` puts in the box, while `.npmrc: "${NPMRC}"` writes the whole stored file, into the box's home in every cell. A whole reference that resolves to nothing — nothing answers for it, or what answers is empty — is never written as an empty file: it is not written at all, and the run starts with the gap logged (`sandbox_env_unresolved`, naming `<step>:<path>`) exactly as an `env` value that does not resolve is left out ([below](#credentials-and-the-run-environment)); where the step lists the path under its `optional_keys`, the file is left out quietly. The same rule holds for a file on a seat's own `sandbox.setup`. A file whose whole body is the seat's own GitHub App token, left out because the app could not issue one yet, is written by the first poll after it can ([The git-auth recipe](#the-git-auth-recipe)).

**A tool a step installs for the run goes in `$HOME/.crewlet/bin`**, the box's own bin directory, which the run's wrapper puts first on the harness's PATH (inside the login shell, after the profile, so a profile cannot replace it). It is under the box's home rather than a system path because a `direct` box runs as the engine's unprivileged user, and two boxes there would otherwise overwrite each other's tools.

### The git-auth recipe

Injecting a code-host token is necessary but not sufficient: a headless `git clone https://github.com/...` has no way to *supply* it and dies with `could not read Username`. The recommended wiring is a config recipe — a credential helper plus rewrites, shipped as an ordinary engine-wide step:

```yaml
providers:
  sandbox:
    setup:
      - name: git-auth
        commands:
          - mkdir -p "$HOME/.local/bin"
          - |
            cat > "$HOME/.local/bin/git-credential-crewlet" <<'SH'
            #!/bin/sh
            [ "$1" = "get" ] || exit 0
            token="$GITHUB_TOKEN"
            file="$HOME/.config/crewlet/github-token"
            [ -s "$file" ] && token=$(cat "$file")
            [ -n "$token" ] || exit 0
            ok=""
            while IFS= read -r line; do
                [ -z "$line" ] && break
                [ "$line" = "host=github.com" ] && ok=1
            done
            [ -n "$ok" ] || exit 0
            echo "username=x-access-token"
            echo "password=$token"
            SH
          - chmod +x "$HOME/.local/bin/git-credential-crewlet"
          - 'git config --global credential."https://github.com".helper "!''$HOME/.local/bin/git-credential-crewlet''"'
          - 'git config --global --add url."https://github.com/".insteadOf "git@github.com:"'
          - 'git config --global --add url."https://github.com/".insteadOf "ssh://git@github.com/"'
          - 'git config --global user.name "$CREWLET_AGENT_HANDLE"'
          - 'git config --global user.email "$CREWLET_AGENT_EMAIL"'
        env:
          GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT: "0"
        brief: >-
          When a GitHub token is available, git authenticates to github.com
          with it automatically: clone, fetch and push over plain HTTPS, SSH
          remotes are rewritten, and the token is read fresh at every call, from
          $HOME/.config/crewlet/github-token or else $GITHUB_TOKEN. For the gh
          harness, pass the current token on each call: GH_TOKEN="$(cat
          "$HOME/.config/crewlet/github-token" 2>/dev/null || printf %s
          "$GITHUB_TOKEN")" gh pr create ... Without a token, public
          repositories still clone anonymously and nothing you push is
          accepted. Never embed a token in a URL or print it. Your git commit
          identity is preconfigured.

```

A seat on at GitHub needs nothing more: Crewlet puts its app's token in its box
as `GITHUB_TOKEN` and in `$HOME/.config/crewlet/github-token`, the file the
helper reads first ([An agent's own integration credentials](#an-agents-own-integration-credentials)).

**The token is the agent's own GitHub App's**, issued by the engine at launch
and good for at least 50 minutes in the environment
([Acting as the app](../integrations/github.md#acting-as-the-app-the-seats-token)).
A run is never stopped by its box's TTL, so it can outlive that, and a
process's environment cannot change under it. What can is a file: the box's
token file, and any setup file whose whole body is the seat's
`${GITHUB_APP_TOKEN_<HANDLE>}`, is rewritten by the
completion poll every time the token behind it is renewed — the launching
node's own poll for a `direct` or `container` box, the fleet's for an `e2b`
one — and by the launch of a job that continues in its turn's kept box, before
that job starts: no poll reaches a box while it is paused for the reviewer or
for a person's answer, and a kept box runs no setup. So the helper above, which
reads the file at every git call, always offers a token GitHub accepts. Each
renewed token is **recorded before the file is written**, beside the run's
record with what its launch handed the box, so a renewed token is scrubbed from
every capture of the run's session taken once its job has ended, as a token the
launch handed is ([Each launch's session, kept](#each-launchs-session-kept)); a
renewal the record does not take is written by a later poll, once one does. The
file is **replaced whole**, never rewritten in place: the new token is written
beside it under a fresh name and renamed over it (on an `e2b` box, uploaded and
then moved by envd), so the helper reads the token from before the renewal or
after it, never an empty or half-written file. A run launched before the seat's app was
installed starts without the token (said as `sandbox_env_unresolved`) and
without the file, and the first poll after the app can issue one writes it.
The helper falls back to `$GITHUB_TOKEN` where the box holds no file, which is
the shape for a seat off at GitHub that declares a personal access token, one
that does not expire within the run, in its `role.sandbox.env`.

Each piece is load-bearing: the helper is **scoped to the code host twice** (the credential config key *and* the `host=` check in the script), so the token can never be offered to a foreign host such as a malicious submodule URL; it reads the token at git-runtime, from the file the engine keeps current or else from the env, and stays silent without one, so public clones fall through to anonymous; the `insteadOf` rewrites use `--add` because the key is multi-valued (without it the second value replaces the first and scp-style `git@…:` remotes stay SSH, failing on host-key verification in a keyless box); and commit identity comes from the engine's generic `$CREWLET_AGENT_*` facts, so commits attribute to the seat without the recipe hardcoding a name. An engine-wide step reaches every agent seat's box, including a seat that declares no token, so its `brief` is written for both: a brief that promised every box a token would send a tokenless seat's run pushing with nothing to push as. And the helper lives in the **box's home**, the one place every cell lets a setup step write it (see [Local sandboxes](#local-sandboxes)), and is written by a command rather than a `files:` entry, though a relative `files:` path would land in the same place: git has to be given the helper's absolute path, which only the box's shell knows (`"$HOME"`, expanded), and the helper has to be made executable, both of which are commands. It is registered as a shell snippet whose path is quoted, `"!'$HOME/…'"`, because git runs a helper's value through the shell and a box's home may hold a space: a `direct` box's sits under `local.state_dir` (by default `~/.crewlet/sandboxes`), and a relative one, like the examples' `./nimbus-data/sandboxes`, is resolved against wherever the engine was started. Written this way, the one step works in every cell.

The GitLab form of this recipe — same shape, `gitlab.com` scoping, `oauth2` username, MRs opened via `git push -o merge_request.create` push options — is in [GitLab Integration](../integrations/gitlab.md#how-code-authoring-works); the block above is itself the GitHub form, and [GitHub Integration](../integrations/github.md#how-code-authoring-works) covers the rest of a GitHub seat's code authoring. The repo to work in is **not** role config — it is task context: the turn's ask names it, and the run clones it inside the box with the seat's injected token. This recipe is for **other hosts** only: a repository on Gitlet is given to a run by the engine, never by a recipe (next section).

---

## Repositories on Gitlet

A run can be given repositories on [Gitlet](gitlet.md), the engine's own git host (`code.backend: gitlet`). Unlike a repository on GitHub or GitLab — task context the turn's ask names and the run clones with a token the seat's configuration declares — a repository on Gitlet is a **grant**. A run is launched with none: its agent checks each repository out as it needs it, calling `code_checkout` over [the tool bridge](#the-tool-bridge--a-seats-own-tools-from-inside-a-box), one repository a call, and the engine resolves it for the seat and writes it on the run's record before the call answers: the repository's id beside its name (so a rename mid-run cannot move the grant), the commit the run starts from, the branch it works on, whether it may push (`read` or `write`), and an optional cone of directories. The record is what the box's access to Gitlet is judged by on every request for as long as the job runs, so a repository is reachable from the moment its checkout answers, and one the run never checked out never is. It binds the box's **git** and nothing else: the run's calls of the seat's own tools through [the tool bridge](#the-tool-bridge--a-seats-own-tools-from-inside-a-box) are the seat's, held to the seat's grant and to a person's stop whatever the run was given, so a run given a repository to read limits its box's git, not the code tools the seat itself holds. A run is given at most 8 repositories, a cone names at most 64 directories, each relative to the repository's root, clean and at most 255 bytes, and a checkout past them is refused before anything is written. A job's repositories are that job's: a run that carries on after a person's answer, in the asking job's box or from its branch in a fresh one, keeps the checkouts of the job it continues, and any other later launch of the turn starts with none. The record also keeps the position on the code log each checkout was resolved at — what the box has been shown — which only ever moves forward. Checking a repository out again on the same branch with the same access answers the grant the record holds, for a box that lost its clone, with commands that clone the branch where it stands now: its head, with whatever the job before it pushed there, read at a position the record's floor is raised to — or, where nothing was pushed and the branch does not exist yet, the commit the first checkout started from. Another branch or another access of it is refused, since one run works on one branch of a repository.

**What a checkout names.** `code_checkout` is offered on the run's own surface, beside `ask_and_wait`, where the company hosts code; it takes one repository, `{repo, base, branch, access, paths, change, open_change}`, every key and type read strictly, since what it names is a grant, and answers the commands that clone it into the box's working directory and the branch to work on:

| Field | What it is | Left out |
|---|---|---|
| `repo` | The repository's name, as `code_list_repos` lists it. A name a rename left redirecting resolves to the repository it names now, and the run is given it by its current name | Required |
| `branch` | The branch the run works on, which the box creates at its base when it does not exist | The seat's own branch for the work item its turn is charged to, `seats/<handle>/<the item's key>` — so every run on one item works on one branch, and a second run continues what the first pushed — or `seats/<handle>/run-<the last 12 hex digits of the turn's id>` for a turn on no work item. A key is spelled for a ref: every character outside letters, digits, `_` and `-` becomes `-`, and a key that had to be respelled (or already ends like one) takes a dash and twelve hex digits of its own digest, so two keys that spell alike are two branches. A work item whose key changes starts a new branch |
| `base` | The branch, tag or commit — a whole id or its first seven or more hex digits — to start from | The branch's head where it exists, else the default branch's; an empty repository has none, and the run's first commit starts its branch |
| `access` | `read` (fetch only) or `write` (and push) | `write` where the box could push the branch — the seat's grant lets it, and in a turn a colleague woke, the branch is the seat's own or one the colleague could push too — else `read`: a read-only seat naming a repository to look at is not refused for it |
| `paths` | A cone of directories to check out, for a large repository | The whole tree |
| `change` | A [change](gitlet.md) to work on, by its number (`42`) or its reference (`web!42`): the run works on the change's source branch, from the head it holds, so its pushes are the change's next revisions — never beside `branch` or `base`, which it decides. Read once, linearizable; a closed or merged change is refused | No change: the branch above |
| `open_change` | `{target, title, draft}` — open a change from the run's branch once the run is done: see [A run's changes](#a-runs-changes). Needs `write`, and never beside `change`, whose branch heads one already | No change opened |

The repositories are resolved as the seat's own code tools resolve theirs — every read at the seat's level, judged by the seat's grant on the node that answers, and, in a turn somebody woke, by **that person's or colleague's grant too**: a box is never given a repository its requester could not read, and a turn a stranger, a person who may not act or a person without the Gitlet permission woke is given none — the checkout is refused, naming Gitlet for the person without it, before anything is read ([ADR-0110](../../adr/0110-reaching-a-repository-takes-the-gitlet-permission.md)). In a turn a colleague seat woke, the run's record names that colleague, and Gitlet holds the box to the colleague's grant on every request it makes — what the box may fetch, and what it may push outside the seat's own `seats/<handle>/`, which stays the seat's own work ([Gitlet → Coding boxes](gitlet.md#coding-boxes)).
