- `status` uses the record's own vocabulary: `launching`, `running`, `awaiting_clarification`, `reseed` or `answered` (a reply is recorded and the engine is resuming the run with it). A `resumed` run is over, because its turn has already taken back the result, so it is not listed. The panel used to write `awaiting_input`, a status the record cannot hold, so the dashboard never saw a live run as waiting on a person.
- `owner` is the node driving the run.
- `paused_at` is the pause reaper's own reading of when the box began being held.

Every node reads the same fleet-wide record, so every node's panel shows the fleet's runs. The dashboard's **Executors** screen reads the record directly (`executors`). A run whose box has already been reclaimed is therefore still listed there, with the row's own facts that the panel does not carry.

### Watching a run live

Nothing a running job writes leaves its box until the run is collected, when its transcript — held to the record's bound, its start and its end — rides the launch's own `executor_collected` record. While it runs, its live output is a **question**, asked only while somebody looks: `executor_tail{turn_id, launch_id}` (`GET /executors/{turn_id}/tail?launch_id=…`). There is no event and no row behind it.

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant D as Dashboard
    participant A as API node
    participant O as Owning node
    participant B as Box
    D->>A: executor_tail{turn_id, launch_id, epoch, after, digest}
    A->>A: read the run's record (launching? not running? who owns it?)
    A->>O: ask on crewlet.observe.executor_tail, with the asker's budget
    alt the reading was read in the last 2 s
        O-->>A: the text after `after`, from the shared reading
    else
        O->>B: attach without waking, then read what each stream grew
        B-->>O: new complete lines
        O->>O: decode, settle and redact, then append to the reading
        O-->>A: the text after `after` — or a reset carrying the last 256 KiB
    end
    A-->>D: {outcome: tail, output: {text, start, end, epoch, digest, reset, held}}
    D->>D: append (or replace on a reset), then trim the front past 256 KiB
```

- **It names the job, not only the run.** A turn can launch more than one job, so `executor_launched` carries the job's `launch_id` (the one the run's record holds and its `executor_collected` record repeats) and the instant the store recorded the launch (`started_at`). A request naming only the turn would show whichever job the record holds now.
- **The node that owns the run answers it, from one reading per job.** Every node serves `crewlet.observe.executor_tail`; the asker reads the run's record, and the incarnation it names as the owner (`PendingRun.Owner`) — or, for a `direct` or `container` box, the node that launched it (`launch_node`), the one node that can reach that box whoever holds the seat now — re-reads the record and answers from its **reading** of the job (`Runner.Follow`): the transcript the harness's decoder builds from the event stream, or the error stream while the event stream has said nothing yet (a harness that failed to start), read through a box attached without waking it (`Provider.Attach`). A reading begins at the start of each stream's last mebibyte, on a line, and every read after takes only what was written since — a ranged read of the stream's end sized to what it grew. When that mebibyte holds no line break at all — a run writing a line longer than the window, `\r`-only progress or a minified blob — the reading has no line to begin after, so it DROPS the rest of that one line, up to the first break that lands after it, the way a partial first line always is, and begins showing from there — rather than show it as a whole line redacted with none of the text before it, which could have put the tail of a credential straddling the window's edge on screen. Those bytes are not counted as held, since nothing will ever show them; the reading says instead that it began after the stream's start (`front`), which is what that line is. The error stream's reading also reads the **64 KiB before** where it begins, with that dropped line's rest after them, and never shows them: they are what its first text is redacted with, so a private key whose BEGIN came before the reading is redacted as the key it is rather than read from inside — which publishes its lines above three that part it — and the reading shows only what they settle, from the line after anything they decide. The event stream's reading needs none, since an event is one line and so is a credential inside one. Nothing in the box is written, signalled or cleared.
- **What it shows is settled.** A line is shown only once nothing written after it can change how it redacts (`redact.Settled`): a line still being written waits for its line break, a private key's BEGIN and the lines under it wait while what comes next could still be the key's body or its END — so a mention of the armour line waits only for the next line, which is not a key's — a line that reads like a key's body — base64 as wide as a key's line, in upper and lower case both — waits until two lines in a row that cannot be one follow it with two more lines after them (four such lines in a row, most often, and five for a run exactly as wide as a key's line, 64, 70 or 76 characters, which an END can read back to across two lines — counted again above a line that could be OpenPGP's checksum, which an END reads back from as from itself), since an END written next could read it back — and a password key waits for the line that decides its value: the value's own, past the wrapping's blank lines, or the next one holding a key or not ending as the key's line did. Not where that line only confirms what the key's line gave on its own, so a run of `password:"` lines is shown as it is written rather than held back whole. A key followed on its line by punctuation and then more — `msg="Enter password:" level=info`, whose quote may be a wrapping's — waits for the next line too, and so does a key a quote closes whose separator ends its line (`case "password":`): one line. Over the 53 files of Go's source tree and x/crypto that hold a password's key, read as written, numbered or as a diff, 14 or 15 lines wait one line longer than they did before a value was read on a later line, by at most 84 bytes. A hash in hex or a path in lower case is in one case and is shown at once; a Go test's name, a CamelCase identifier or a path in mixed case as wide as a key's line is in both, with or without a digit, and waits like a key's line, so a verbose test run's output can arrive in bursts, held as far back as 64 KiB until lines that are no key's release it. A line holding a run that reads like a piece of a key anywhere on it — eight characters or more in both cases with a digit, which an END written next could read back to as a line a cut wrote into — waits too, until four lines that hold none follow it. Fed `go test -v` output of Crewlet's own packages a line at a time, a few hundred bytes wait on average and at most about 4 KiB. So what a viewer sees, read by read, is exactly the whole text redacted — never a key's body that its END line, a moment later, would have hidden. What is held back is counted (`held`), so the screen can say why a line has not appeared.
- **One read of the box answers everybody watching.** The owner reads a job's box at most once every two seconds (`sandbox.LiveReuse`), however many people have the run open, and requests that arrive while a read is in flight wait for that one. Each answer waits for the box at most three quarters of its asker's budget (the request carries it), so a request the asker gave up on frees its answer slot instead of holding it for as long as a slow box takes; the read itself goes on, under its own budget of one completion-poll interval, and the next request is answered from what it found — or, if the box is still being read, from the last read there was, stamped with when it was taken. A reading nobody has asked about for 30 seconds is dropped by the owner's own sweep, every ten seconds, whether or not anything asks it anything again — which in a fleet nothing does once a run stops: the node serving the dashboard reads the run's record, answers `not_running` itself and never asks the owner again, and a viewer who closes the page asks nobody.
- **A viewer is sent only what it lacks.** Every request says what the asker holds — the reading's `epoch`, the byte offset it holds through (`after`) and the `digest` the owner gave it there, all three absent on a first request, which holds nothing — and is answered with the text after that offset, its `start` and `end` offsets beside it — always both, `0` included, since a run that has settled nothing yet is answered at `0` and followed from it. Anything else is a **reset** carrying the last 256 KiB in whole lines (the most a viewer holds, the record's own bound): an epoch that names another reading (the owner restarted, the reading began again further on, the account switched from the error stream to the transcript), a digest that does not match the window the asker holds (a node on a build that derives the stream differently), or a viewer more than 256 KiB behind. A run whose owner moves to a node on the same build **continues**: the new owner's reading of the same stream from the same place derives the same text, and the digest says so. A `direct` or `container` box is read on the node that launched it whoever holds the seat, so its run is followed there across a move of the seat with no reset, and a launching node that does not answer is `owner_silent`, naming that node.
- **Six outcomes, never an empty answer standing in for another.** `tail` is the output (possibly empty: a run that has written nothing yet says so). `launching` is a job whose box is still being made and provisioned — not a run that stopped, and a screen keeps asking. `not_running` names the record's own status — parked on a question, `replaced` by a later job on the same run, or no record at all because the run was collected. `owner_silent{node}` is the node that reads the run's box — its owner, or a `direct` or `container` box's launching node, held or not — not answering inside the two-second fleet read budget (one that is draining or whose heartbeat lapsed is asked like any other, and its silence is this), and it names that node; or an `e2b` run no node holds right now, which names none. `box_paused` is a running record whose box is paused where its backend cannot read it without waking it — the moment between a collection pausing the box and the record moving on — and the box is left paused. `box_gone` is a running record whose box its backend says is gone for good: its provider reaped it, or it's a local box whose root didn't outlive its node. Nothing more is read from it, and its waiter records its job lost, so its round is carried on in a fresh run (see *A job lost with its host* under [Durability](#durability-completion-and-observability)), and a screen keeps asking until the record says so. A box the owner couldn't read is an error, and what crosses from the owner is its class, never its text: the owner logs the failure (`sandbox_tail_failed`, the class as `failure`, at warn for a fault and for a class no wait clears and at debug otherwise), whether it is answering a peer or the person asked it directly, and the asker answers the class. The surface that answers the person writes its own line about the answer at debug, because the failure is in the owner's log already; an owner on a newer build that names a class this one doesn't know is answered as a fault, and the asker logs `sandbox_tail_failure_unknown` naming it. A provider that didn't answer or is at its limit, and an owner that couldn't answer just now (the run's seat moving off it, the node stopping, its coordination store not answering), are `unavailable` with a `Retry-After`; a sandbox setup somebody has to fix, a refused key or an unpaid account are `unavailable` with none, by the [classes a launch is told by](#when-a-run-does-not-start); and a fault of the owner's own is `query_failed`, whose detail is in the owner's log.
- **The dashboard asks every three seconds, and only while somebody is looking**: while a running run's span is open on the turn trace, or while the run's own page (Live › Executors, `#/live/executors/{turn_id}`) is open on a running job. Each request is chained after the last answer, so one open view has one request in flight; closing it or the run ending stops the poll, and so does a failed read the engine says no wait clears (a sandbox setup somebody has to fix, a refused key) until the socket reconnects or the view is opened again. The run's page asks by the `launch_id` the run board names (`executors` serves it), so it shows the job the row holds now, and when a later job replaces it the page reads the board again and follows the new one.
- **After collection the record is the turn's.** A collected run's row is deleted, and its page reads the turn instead: every `executor_collected` the turn's launches published — what each job reported, what it delivered, its tokens and its transcript — under the task its `executor_launched` carried. A link to a run that has finished still lands on what it did.

### Reading a seat's sessions

**A run's session is kept after it, and read back as its transcript.** Every
capture a collection takes — the archive the run packed when its harness
exited ([Claude Code](#a-claude-code-runs-session),
[OpenCode](#an-opencode-runs-session)) — is sealed into the fleet's
[object store](object-store.md) and named by its turn's row of the `executor`
log
([ADR-0073](https://github.com/crewlet/crewlet/blob/main/adr/0073-a-seats-executor-session-is-a-sealed-object-its-turns-row-names.md),
and [Conversation Sessions](conversation-sessions.md#configuration) for how
long and how much is kept). That archive is the whole record of what the run
read, called and wrote, and a seat's **Sessions** (under the Memory section of
its page's Activity tab on the dashboard, [A seat's profile](../reference/dashboard-design.md#a-seats-profile),
or [`GET /agents/{id}/sessions`](../reference/api-endpoints.md#get-agentsidsessions))
list them, the latest begun first: how each turn ended (the engine's own
account, which the transcript cannot know), what it was asked, and whether its
session is still kept. **A row begins at its turn's first launch** where the
turn read its conversation — its `begin`, which names the session that launch
was handed (`base_turn`) — and at its first capture otherwise. **And a row goes
with the turn that was handed it**: once a later turn of its conversation that
was handed its session, and that kept a session of its own, ends, its end
absorbs the turns its read handed out, and their rows are gone from the list —
as are rows past the retention, and a removed seat's. A session read by its turn
after its row went is `404 not_found`, saying it may have gone any of these ways.
**Reading a session holds nothing**, so a session can go while it is read, and
is said as released rather than as a loss: see below.

**A row that keeps no session says why**, one of four: nothing of the turn's
session was captured (yet, while it runs); its newest capture was not kept, and
none before it was — its end said so, with the reason the store or the log gave,
redacted; an end an earlier version of the engine recorded took an older capture
off the row too, where one of this version keeps it and says the turn
[went on past it](turn-engine.md#how-a-turn-ends-on-the-executor-log);
a run [ran out of context](conversation-sessions.md#a-session-that-ran-out-of-context-is-retired)
in its session, which retired it; or the seat's session budget released it to
make room for newer ones — a session its turn went on past included, which only
the budget takes off, said with the newer capture that was not kept beside it.

**A live turn's row that names a session is in progress** (`in_progress`): the
newest session its runs have kept — a job's final capture as its collection
took it, while the turn goes on to its review or waits on a person's answer, or
one a loss left its box to take — which its run may have gone on past, and
whose last call may have no result yet. It is read as the turn's session, never
as an ended turn's, and a relaunch continues it only where its `continuable`
says so: a relaunch continues the newest kept capture of the job it follows,
and a live turn's row often names an earlier job's. Every reader is told so in
one sentence of the engine's (`in_progress_note`), its words alone: "This turn
has not ended: this is the newest session its runs have kept, captured at
`<captured_at>`; its run may have gone on past it, and its last call may have
no result yet." **A row kept up to a session its turn went on past says so**
(`kept_note`): that the session was taken while the turn still worked and that
what the turn did after is not in it — or, of a turn that was lost, that it may
not hold the last of what the turn did — with the newer capture that was not
kept and why, redacted, where its end names one; that reason quotes what
refused the capture, so a seat reads the note fenced, as it reads the row's
other words. A row that holds its turn's last word says neither, unless its
turn was lost, which every lost turn's row says. Both are read off the row
alone: nothing on the log says what kind of capture landed a row, and no reader
of it reads the turn's run record.

**Each says which launch would continue it** (`continued_by`), **whether it
would** (`continuable`), where (`continues_in`), and where not, why
(`not_continuable`), each decided by the one decision that launch takes, over
what that launch reads, and of the member of the seat's executor chain it would
be launched on now — the first not sitting out its plan — with the cell its run
lands in and its runner:

- **A live turn's session, by a relaunch of it** (`relaunch`): the decision a
  relaunch takes ([above](#a-relaunch-continues-the-turns-session)), over the
  turn's row of the executor log as a relaunch reads it, the turn's run record
  in the coordination store and the job that record names as the one a relaunch
  would follow. `any_box` where the turn is live and not ending, the newest
  capture of that job was kept, states its save point and is the one the row
  names, and that runner restores its harness, format and release; a round
  whose job was lost a second time is carried on in a session of its own,
  which a reader is told cannot be said where a review of the work it
  submitted may yet continue it. `parked_box`
  where that is not so but a person's answer would still carry the turn's
  parked run on in the box it parked in — the run still holds the box, the box
  ran the row's own session (not one it started in that one's place, and not
  one whose job's box never said which of the two it ran), the member is of the
  box's harness in its cell, and a box on the engine host is on the host of the
  node holding the seat, which alone can continue in it — which continues the
  session that box holds there and nowhere else, for as long as the box is
  held; `not_continuable` then says why no other box would. A box that cannot
  be continued in says why, beside why the kept capture is not continued. **A
  turn that has kept no session of its own** — its first job lost with its box
  before anything of it was kept — says so, and where its row still holds the
  session its first launch was handed and the member restores it, that a
  relaunch in a box of its own continues that session instead
  ([A conversation's next turn continues its last turn's
  session](#a-conversations-next-turn-continues-its-last-turns-session)).
- **An ended turn's session, by its conversation's next turn** (`next_turn`):
  the decision that turn's first launch takes of the head its dispatch reads.
  Only the conversation's **head** is continued — of its ended turns whose rows
  still name a session, the one that read the conversation last ([A
  conversation's next turn continues its last turn's
  session](#a-conversations-next-turn-continues-its-last-turns-session)), which
  the read that answered the row names from
  the same snapshot, so a row's standing is the one its page's `read_level`
  states — `any_box`, where the member restores it, and in a box of the next
  turn's own. A row that is not the head names the turn whose session the next
  turn is handed instead; one that names no session says why; and one whose
  company keeps no session says so. A turn whose run is over, or whose ending is
  decided, and whose end is not on the log as the row was read is the next
  turn's too, and `null` until its end lands. A turn that served no
  conversation — a colleague's ask, a generic source's wake — is continued by
  no launch: it sends no `continued_by`, and `continuable` is `false`.
- **An ended fire's session, by its schedule's next fire** (`next_fire`): the
  same decision as `next_turn`, for a row of a schedule's conversation — a
  schedule is a conversation of each seat that runs it
  ([above](#a-conversations-next-turn-continues-its-last-turns-session)) — told
  in the schedule's words: a fire that is not its schedule's head names the
  fire whose session the next fire is handed instead. A live fire's row is a
  relaunch's like any other's, and where its turn has kept nothing it names the
  last fire a relaunch in a box of its own would continue.

**The member is the one the node holding the seat would pick**, off the chain
of the company revision the launch runs on: which members sit out a spent plan
is what that node's own calls have met, which company revision says sessions are
kept is the revision it runs, a resumed segment of the turn running there
relaunches — its reviewer's send-back, the round it carries on — on the revision
it was admitted under, whatever an apply has put live since, and a
conversation's next turn is admitted under the revision live when it comes. So
the node answering asks it — itself where it holds the seat, and otherwise the
node the seat's lease names, once a page — and is answered, session by session,
with that node's pick and whether that member's runner restores the session: a
live turn's by its turn, and a conversation's head by none, so a segment of the
ended turn still pinned in the moments before it returns answers for no
revision the next turn runs on. As far as the engine can know before a box is
chosen, and no further: whether the box a launch gets runs a harness release
that can take the session up, whether it is a box of the launch's own, and
whether the seat's budget can carry its context, are the launch's to find out,
and no row says them.

**An unknown is never a no**: `continuable` is null, with its reason, where the
run record or the row at the relaunch's level could not be read, and where the
launch would not decide now either — the job a relaunch would follow still
working, or a claim on it whose read is still under way, one that has neither
taken the job's final capture nor begun its turn; that job's capture still being
kept; what the launch is launched on not to be said (every member of the seat's
chain sitting out its plan, no node holding the seat, the node holding it not
answering within two seconds); a relaunch only the resume of a collected job
decides, until its turn begins — whether its round is carried on in its box,
which it is where the job's sign-in was replaced while it ran, and whether a
round lost a second time is carried on in a session of its own or reviewed from
the work it submitted; and a turn whose end is not on the log yet. **A resumed
turn says what it does with the round as it begins**, on the job's record —
collects the job, or carries the round on — and from then on, its review
included, the claim is decided on that, whether or not its read took any
capture: a turn that collects its job is reviewed, and its send-back continues
nothing in the job's box; one that carries the round on in that box continues it
there. A claim of a person's answer reads no box, and is decided whatever its
job kept.

**What a page costs.** A page reads the coordination store once for its runs,
one listing of the seat's live runs — and one turn's record alone for a live row
the listing does not hold, a record of a status this build does not know or one
it could not decode — and none where every row on it has ended; and once more
for the seat's lease, to find the node holding the seat, wherever a row asks it
— a live row whose run is neither over nor ending, or a conversation's head —
that node's own pages included. Where the store does not answer, a live turn's
row says so, and the page is answered all the same. **A live row is read again
at the relaunch's level** (`linearizable`) only where the page was read below
it and the row names another capture than the one its run's record keeps, or
the record keeps none — a copy of the log behind the record, which would read a
capture a relaunch continues as one the log no longer names: a read at that
level costs a barrier on the executor log, and a row naming the kept capture
decides as one read there would. An ended row is decided on its page's own
snapshot, its conversation's head read in the same transaction as the rows: one
seek of the conversation's line index for each conversation the page names. A
transcript's later pages (read with a `cursor`) leave `continuable` null: its
first page says it.

**One session opens as its transcript**
([`GET /agents/{id}/sessions/{turn_id}`](../reference/api-endpoints.md#get-agentsidsessionsturn_id)):
its object is streamed out of the store and opened under its own associated
data, and the archive is read by the runner that packs it, which alone knows
its layout — Claude Code's transcript, one record a line, then each subagent's
own from its sidecar; OpenCode's export, a message at a time, each read by the
kind OpenCode 2 gives it. The entries are what the run was briefed, the notes a
person sent it while it worked, what its model wrote, every tool call and what
answered it, and the compaction summaries the harness wrote in place of what
came before them; the model's thinking and the harness's bookkeeping are none —
OpenCode's own messages included: its notices in the person's turn, an update
of the run's instructions, a skill or a shell command a person ran in the
session, an execution's end, and a switch of the session's agent, model or
directory. An OpenCode message of a kind this build does not know is said where
it was, as a record of no shape the read knows is. A note is known by the
heading the engine opens every note with, wherever the harness filed it —
OpenCode as a person's message, Claude Code as a hook's context in a record of
its own — and the line
the run's own hook prints as it hands notes over is a note entry too. It is
read a page at a time and bounded — 200 entries and 1 MiB of text a page,
16 KiB an entry, and 256 MiB a read of the archive as it inflates and, apart,
of what a tar's members hand out, so an archive a box packed to inflate without
end, or with a sparse member that declares more than it holds, stops there —
and every entry's text is **redacted at this read before it is cut**, whatever
the capture did, and is always an opening of what its whole text redacts to:
all of it that can reach the entry's bound and a private key's length past it
is redacted inside as much again, so a credential that begins inside the bound
is redacted whole, and nothing the read could not settle is handed out however
much the redaction shrinks what comes before it.
An object the store no longer holds, one that does not open under the fleet's
keyring, an archive that is not one its format describes and one this build
does not read are each said as such, in the engine's own words, beside the
turn's ending, which the row still holds.

**A session released while it is read is said as released.** A read writes
nothing and keeps no session past what the seat's budget and the retention
give it — a hold taken by a read would be a write every reader makes, and it
would keep the budget from releasing the reader's own next capture — so between
the read of a session's row and the end of its object's stream a later turn's
end can absorb it, the budget release it, the end of a turn that [ran out of
context](conversation-sessions.md#a-session-that-ran-out-of-context-is-retired)
in it retire it, the retention delete it or its turn capture it again, and the
collector take the object. Read alone, that object
is missing, or, taken while it streams, one whose bytes do not open. So a read
whose object fails reads the turn's row once more, at `linearizable`, and says
what the row says now: no row, that the row went while the session was read
and nothing more of it can be read; a row naming no object, its reason and the
same; a row naming a newer capture, that capture read from its start on a first
page, and a later page's cursor refused as one captured again — of a turn that
has not ended, as one whose runs have kept its session again, which is true
whichever capture moved it, and never that a run saved it. Only where the
row still names the object is the failure the store's or the archive's own, and
the row is read again once. What the read already handed out stands: every byte
of it came from a chunk that authenticated.

**A node decodes two transcripts at a time.** One read can hold a line of the
harness's record at its 32 MiB bound, as much again while the buffer gathering
it grows, and the copy decoding it makes — on the order of 100 MiB — and a node
answers every dashboard tab open on it and every REST caller, each with a read
of its own. So each node decodes at most two transcripts at once
(`TranscriptReadsAtOnce`), about 200 MiB at their worst, whichever socket or
route asks. A read past them waits for a slot before the session's object is
opened — an opened object would hold its store's connection while it waited —
for as long as its caller waits, holding no decoder meanwhile, and a slot frees
within one bounded read; a caller that leaves first is answered nothing, and
nothing was opened for it.

**It is the seat's trail, and it holds more than the run's live output
showed.** It is read by exactly who reads that output — the seat, whoever leads
it, and an administrator, `audit.seat.read`
([Identity and Access](identity-and-access.md#the-authority-table-one-function-decides))
— and by nobody it was withheld from. But the live output names a call's
subject on one line and a failed result's first line, and never a successful
result's body; a session's transcript holds every call's whole input and each
result's text to an entry's bound — the contents of the files the run read and
what its commands printed — and every subagent's thread. The redaction matches
the credential shapes the engine knows, not values, so a secret of no
recognisable shape that a run read is in its transcript as the run read it.

#### A seat reads its own

**A seat's executor finds and reads its seat's other sessions itself**, with
two built-in tools on the seat's registry, each a read of the seat's rows on
the executor log at the seat's own level (`linearizable`):

- **`list_sessions`** lists the seat's sessions, the latest begun first, ten
  to a page and at most ten (a larger `limit` is read as ten), the most whose
  page fits one 64 KiB tool answer with every field of every row at its
  bound, which comes to 5,994 bytes a row, so an eleventh does not fit: each
  row's turn and conversation,
  whether it is the reading turn's own (`this_turn`, its earlier launches) or
  of the conversation that turn serves (`this_conversation`), when it was
  captured and ended, whether its session is still kept and its size kept
  (`kept`, `kept_bytes`), whether it is in progress (`in_progress`, with the
  engine's sentence for one, `in_progress_note`) and what a person is told of
  a session its turn went on past (`kept_note`, above), the session its first
  launch was handed (`base_turn`), and what its turn was asked and how it
  ended, each of those words cut to 300 bytes. **With `query`** it keeps
  the sessions whose words —
  what the turn was asked, its conversation, and its ending's accounts — hold
  every word, matched whole as the engine's [search](../guides/search.md)
  matches a word (case folded, no stemming) against what the row's redaction
  shows of its own words, so a word found only inside a redacted credential, or
  only in the marker the redaction wrote in its place, finds nothing. The
  node holding the rows examines a thousand of them a page and answers its
  hits alone, so a node without `data` is never shipped a seat's history to
  filter; and a page says how many sessions it searched and whether they
  reached the seat's oldest (`searched`), never that nothing matches past
  them — nor among them, where the node that answered holds records it could
  not read (`incomplete`): that page says sessions may be missing from it.
- **`read_session`** reads a page of one session's transcript by its turn id:
  on its first page the row whole, then its entries, each with its place among
  every entry the transcript holds (`ordinal`). A seat's page is held to fifty
  entries, 32 KiB of their text and 8 KiB an entry — a quarter, a
  thirty-second and a half of a person's — and trimmed from its end to fit one
  tool answer, its `next_cursor` naming the first entry left out. **`kinds`**
  narrows it to some kinds of entry (`["brief","model","summary"]` for the
  gist), and **`match`** to the entries whose whole text holds every word —
  a tool call's read as the JSON its input is written in, through its
  strings' escapes, so the word that opens a line of a command, or of a file
  the call wrote, is the call's word and not glued to the escaped line break
  before it — decided on what the redaction shows of the entry's own words,
  so a word only a redacted credential held, or only its marker spells, is no hit, whether
  asked alone or beside a word the entry shows, and a guess at a redacted value
  is answered as a wrong one is, down to where a page full of hits stops: at
  the next hit. Each hit is handed out as the window of its
  redacted text around the first word it shows, cut never inside a marker,
  with where the window begins in that redacted text (`text_from`) and a
  cursor that reads the transcript on from that entry. Every entry states the
  size of what its whole text redacts to (`bytes`) — of a text longer than
  the read redacts, its size as recorded rounded up to a KiB — never its size
  as recorded to the byte, which beside a text shown whole told the seat how
  long each value the redaction took out was. A part of the record
  the read could not read (an entry of kind `unread`) is handed out whatever
  `kinds` or `match` asks, never passed over. A page says what it searched: a
  read that reached the transcript's end, that no entry from where it began
  holds the words — or, beside the parts it could not read, that it cannot say
  what those held; one the archive's read budget stopped, where.

**Only the seat's own, by construction.** Neither tool takes a seat: both read
the seat of the turn the call is made in, which over the
[tool bridge](#the-tool-bridge--a-seats-own-tools-from-inside-a-box) is the
run's own seat whatever the request carried, so another seat's turn id is
answered exactly as one nobody has. The reviewer is offered neither — it judges
the turn's own record, never the executor's memory — no person's surface
registers them, and a person, or their assistant, calling one is refused as an
agent seat's own verb: a person reads a seat's sessions as its trail, above. A
[worker](turn-engine.md#workers) its parent grants one reads as the seat's own
delegate in the same turn.

**Every word is fenced as a harness's**, under `executor_session` at
`external` trust ([Agent Runtime](agent-runtime.md#text-somebody-else-wrote)):
an entry naming the seat whose run kept it, the row's words naming nobody —
never the reading seat's own, since a transcript quotes whatever its run's
tools returned.

**What a read costs.** One read of the executor log a call, which its read
model counts (150 a seat a day). A transcript is decoded on the node holding
the seat, in one of its two transcript slots, and seats take at most one of
those two between them, so a node whose seats page transcripts still decodes a
person's. A call the object store or the node's other reads hold past two
minutes is answered unavailable, naming the session's size, and never as a
session that holds nothing.

**A read holds nothing.** Reading a session writes no record and holds no
session, so it keeps none past what the seat's budget and the retention give
it. A session released while it is listed or read is said as released, from
what its row says then, as a person's read says it (above), and what was read
of it before stands.

**A run is told of the session it does not continue.** Where a launch read a
kept session it could have continued — the turn's own, or the one its
conversation's last turn left — and its run starts a session of its own
instead, the run's brief names that session and says to read it with
`read_session` ([What a harness is told](#what-a-harness-is-told)). Nothing
else in a prompt mentions the tools: their descriptions say what they read.

**A company that keeps no session offers neither tool**:
`turn_engine.conversation_session.enabled: false` withholds both from every
model, the sessions kept before it went off included, which people still read
as the seat's trail ([Conversation Sessions](conversation-sessions.md#configuration)).
No launch of that revision is offered them, and each call asks the revision
its node runs now, so a launch already running when its node applies it is
refused at its next call — forbidden, saying the switch is off — rather than
reading on until it ends.
Neither is offered on a node with no object store, nor on one whose keyring
cannot open a sealed stream.

---

## Budgets

The harness calls the LLM itself, from inside the box, so the engine cannot gate it request by request as the engine's own tool loop gates the reviewer, workers and onboarding. The model is **floor + cap + post-account**:

- **Pre-flight floor, on every launch.** The turn's first launch and every relaunch — a reviewer's send-back, or a person's answer carrying a parked run on — is refused when the seat's or the company's remaining budget in any capped window is below `turn_engine.executor_min_budget_tokens`, or when the budget cannot be read: launching on an unknown budget is how a company discovers its ceiling by spending past it. **Unset, the floor is 25000 tokens**, because a run's first request alone is the harness's harness prompt and tool definitions — about 15k input tokens for Claude Code — plus the seat's bridged tool array and its system prompt, and a run costs a box, a clone and its setup before that; `0` turns the floor off. Below the floor is a **budget refusal**, which waits rather than fails: on a dispatch the seat is [parked](agent-runtime.md#the-budget-park) until the window turns over or a revision moves a ceiling (`seat_budget_parked` with `starved` set), and a resumed segment is held on the same window, its job kept collected and its seat held. A seat or company `token_budget` ceiling below the floor is refused on the write, because such a seat could never launch. **A relaunch that continues the turn's session is held to more**: the larger of the floor and the session's context with the brief that continues it, since it sends that context before it reads a word of the brief — held and stated on that figure, as a resumed segment is, never parked — and a session whose figure no capped window's ceiling can ever leave room for is not continued at all ([What a continued launch costs](#what-a-continued-launch-costs)).
- **Self-limits, where you set them.** `role.sandbox.max_turns`, else `providers.sandbox.default_max_turns` — uncapped unless one of them is set — becomes the harness's own turn cap on both runners (Claude Code's `--max-turns`, OpenCode's agent `steps`), so a capped run self-limits; an uncapped one is held to the floor at every launch and charged in full when it is collected.
- **A spent seat is not resumed into a refusal.** On a seat no run holds, a person's reply to a run's clarification question is offered to the run only after the [budget park](agent-runtime.md#the-budget-park) has asked whether the seat has room, because resuming the run charges tokens exactly as a turn does. A seat whose window is refusing holds the reply on its inbox until the window turns over, then offers it. A collected run that takes a window past its ceiling parks the seat's next delivery the same way.
- **Post-accounting.** On collect, reported usage is charged to the budget cascade, and the phase event carries the real usage and cost so the Tokens view rolls a run's spend up alongside the phases on this host. Mid-run enforcement is best-effort by design, the price of the capability. OpenCode's tokens and price come from its session's own report ([OpenCode](#opencode)), and a run whose report is missing reports zero rather than an estimate. **A run that continues a session is charged what it added to the session**, never the session's whole spend, which both harnesses report on a continued run: Claude Code's per-run figures, or its result less the one the job before it left ([Claude Code](#claude-code)), and OpenCode's report less the one its run read before it started. **What a run reports is never a refund**: the usage is parsed from what the harness printed inside a box the run's own agent can run any command in, so a negative token count or cost is read as nothing spent (`sandbox_usage_negative`) rather than subtracted from the seat's counter and from every rollup — every runner hands such a figure on as reported, so the floor, and the warning, apply to all of them alike.
- **Charged once per run.** A run's tokens reach the seat's counter and the company's once, however many times its completion is delivered. A resume that fails (the seat moved between the publish and the receive, say) sends the completion back, and the retry collects the same finished job again; the run's coordination record says its spend is already counted, so that retry charges nothing, whether it lands on the same node, on the seat's next owner, or after a restart. The record is written by the same compare-and-swap that hands the completion back, so no retry can find the run reopened without it. A relaunch in the same turn — a reviewer's send-back, or a person's answer carrying the run on — is a second job and is charged on its own. The charge is a **post-charge**: the tokens are already spent, so no cap can refuse them, and both counters record the run in full — in the day, week and month it is collected in, on the company's clock — whatever the ceilings say. A run that takes a counter past a cap is logged as `sandbox_spend_over_budget`, the turn continues regardless, and the next round the seat or the company attempts is refused against the recorded figure — the resumed turn reads the counter once before it calls anything, so its own next round is refused before it is sent rather than billed and then refused. An over-cap charge is recorded like any other, so a retry does not offer it again; only a charge the counter never answered (`sandbox_accounting_failed`) is, and if that one had in fact landed, the retry counts it twice, which trips a cap early rather than late. A charge that reached the **company's** counter and failed on the seat's (`sandbox_accounting_partial`) keeps the company's share — nothing a counter recorded is taken back — and the run's record says so, so the retry records the seat's share alone rather than counting the company twice. A resume that succeeds after such a charge brings no retry, so that one seat's own counter stays short of the run until its window turns over; the company's stays exact.
- **Charged to the task too.** A turn on a Worklet task charges that task for the runs it detached: the turn segment that resumes from a collected run adds the run's tokens to the task's own spend, beside its own phases, along with the condensing of its report or failure where one was past what the run's record carries (see [Spend is on the task](../guides/worklet.md#spend-is-on-the-task)). A run that stopped to ask a question has no such segment until a person answers, so its tokens and that condensing are recorded with the question on the run's row, and the segment the answer resumes is the one that pays them, whichever node it runs on, however long the question waited. Each segment is recorded once, under the run and launch it resumed, so a retried resume adds nothing. A turn a person stops closes with a segment of its own, which charges only what the segments before it left unpaid: a run's spend read when it was stopped, and what a segment charged to nothing handed on — never again what a segment that charged the task already paid for.

---

## Security model

- **Every run is fully permissioned, and its cell decides what that reaches.** A headless run has nobody to answer a permission prompt, so the harness runs with every permission in every cell — Claude Code under `bypassPermissions`, OpenCode with every action allowed but `question` — and the engine does not filter what it does inside its box, nor which MCP servers its own configuration, the repository's or a plugin's starts there ([MCP inside the sandbox](#mcp-inside-the-sandbox)). What it can reach is the cell's: `e2b` runs it on a fresh remote VM per run, off the engine host altogether, and `container` confines it to a container on the engine host with only the box's own directory mounted. Human gating, where wanted, happens at the merge request — not mid-run.
- **`run_in: direct` runs it on the engine host.** There the harness runs as the engine's own user: per-box state isolation and an allowlisted environment still hold, but the host's files, and whatever that user's credentials reach, are within the run's reach. Pick it only for a dedicated host you are willing to hand to an autonomous agent, for the entry that needs the host itself, or for a first run on your own machine while its seats take work from you alone; pick `container` (or `e2b`) anywhere else, a workstation included, and before a seat takes work from anyone else, with [an image of your own](#bring-your-own-image). It is opt-in twice over — a `local:` block *plus* an explicit `run_in` naming it, on a `providers.llm` entry or as `providers.sandbox.default_run_in` — and because the choice is per entry, a company can run the entry that needs the host login `direct` and point every other seat at an entry whose cell keeps the run off the host. In a company that hosts its own code a `direct` box reads more than the host's files: every repository a Gitlet node's cache holds, whatever its `visibility` or the run's grants, and — on any node, Gitlet node or not — the Tier A keyring every run's credential is derived from, in `crewlet.yaml` and in the engine process's own environment, so it can mint the token of ANY live executor and push as that executor's seat. A refusal of `direct` for a run given repositories would close neither read: both are files of the engine's user whatever the run was given. So `crewlet validate` warns, at the `run_in` that places it, of every `direct` box in such a company; point a seat whose grants must bind it at an entry whose cell is `container` or `e2b`.
- **Only the seat's own identity enters the box.** The injected env carries credentials the agent legitimately acts *as*: its LLM entry's key, with the rest of that entry's `env` (a gateway's token among it), and the external-service tokens its config declares (`role.sandbox.env`). Leaking those from inside the box exposes only what the agent already is. Because the code-host token is the seat's **own** (its GitHub App's installation token, scoped to its tier and repositories, or a PAT of its own), an MR/PR the harness opens is attributed to the agent, not to a shared bot identity. An app's token in a box lives at most an hour, and is renewed only in the files that name it.
- **Shared infrastructure secrets never enter the box.** The OTLP backend's ingest token is the canonical example: the sandbox sees only an engine-fronted, path-token-scoped OTLP endpoint (`CREWLET_SANDBOX_OTEL_RECEIVER_URL`); the engine attaches the real backend credential upstream. Apply the same rule to any credential you add — if the agent doesn't act *as* it, don't inject it.
- **A box URL may be plain `http://`; its run's credentials then cross the network in clear.** The engine accepts `http://` exactly as it accepts `https://` for the URLs a box dials back to it — `CREWLET_BOX_GIT_URL`, `CREWLET_MCP_BRIDGE_URL` and `CREWLET_SANDBOX_OTEL_RECEIVER_URL` — in every cell, and warns about none of them. Each request carries its run's token — as `/box/git/`'s Basic password, or in its path (`/mcp/{token}`, `/otlp/{token}/v1/{signal}`) — so over `http://` the token, and what the request carries, cross in clear every network between the box and the host the URL names. Which networks those are is a property of the URL, not of the cell: a request stays on the box's machine only when the URL names the box's own host — `127.0.0.1` from a `direct` box, `172.17.0.1` or `host.docker.internal` from a `container` one. A remote cell's request (`e2b`, the vendor's cloud or a self-hosted cluster) always crosses networks the deployment does not own, and a local cell's crosses the fleet's own whenever its URL names another node or a balancer — Gitlet's URL does on any fleet whose seat nodes are not its Gitlet nodes (any Gitlet node serves any box, so it names a Gitlet node or a balancer over them), and the receiver's wherever another node serves `/otlp/`. Whoever reads that traffic reads the code the box clones and pushes, the seat's tool calls and their answers and the run's telemetry, and holds each token for its own window: the git token until the run's record ends it — the run ended, reaped or launched again; its seven-day expiry is only a backstop — in which it can fetch what the run was granted and push, as the run's seat, what its grants allow; the bridge's while the run's session lives and at most four hours (`mcpbridge.DefaultTTL`), in which it can call the seat's own tools as the seat; the receiver's until it expires, twice the run's timeout plus its pause allowance after the launch that minted it, in which it can write into the run's telemetry. Serve every box URL whose path leaves the box's host over `https://` — TLS ending at a balancer or a proxy in front of the listener the URL names — which is all three of them for a remote cell, and keep `http://` only for a URL that names the box's own host.
- **A `container` box on a fleet node reaches the fleet's broker, and is refused there — but on a member's host network it can read the fleet's traffic.** A container box joins the network `local.network` names, or the runtime's default bridge, and either reaches the host it runs on: on a fleet, a broker member's route port on the member's own host, and the members' leaf listeners from a leaf's. The embedded broker admits only a connection presenting a key derived from `secrets.keys`, so a box that reaches one is refused like any process outside the fleet (see [Deployment](../guides/deployment.md#the-fleets-broker-admits-only-its-own-nodes)). What it does not do is encrypt: given the HOST's network (`local.network: host`, or a `--network host` run argument) on a member's host, a box shares the member's network namespace, and with Docker's default capabilities it can read the fleet's plaintext links — a route's credential at connect time included — and reach the member's loopback client listener, which admits only the operator's key. Keep coding boxes off a fleet node's host network, or run them in `e2b`. **Beside an external NATS cluster** (`stream.type: nats`) the engine dials only a server that authenticates its clients, so a box holding none of that cluster's credentials is refused there as well (see [Deployment](../guides/deployment.md#an-external-nats-server)).
- **A run's own tokens are redacted from everything it hands back.** Every per-run token the engine mints — the telemetry endpoint's, the tool bridge's, a coding box's git credential, an artifact view ticket's — opens with the literal `crewlet_rt1` (`crewlet_rt1.<tag>.<subject>.<expiry>.<signature>`), and every text a box hands back — its report, its transcript, its question, its failure, its live output — is redacted of every token of that shape, to `[REDACTED:run-token]`, on the node that collects the run, before anything is stored or shown. It is recognised by its shape, as a vendor's key is, because no node that collects a run holds the tokens its launch minted, and a harness that prints its environment or its MCP configuration to debug its setup prints its own.
- **A kept session leaves its box scrubbed.** Every secret the engine put in a box by value — a token the completion poll renewed in it while the run worked included, recorded before it was written, in every session taken once its job had ended — and every credential shape the engine's redaction knows, is taken out of each file of a [kept session](#each-launchs-session-kept) before it is sealed, so a session continued in another box — a remote one included — carries none of the login or the environment its first box was handed.
- **Everything else a run hands back is redacted by shape alone.** Its report, its transcript, its question, its failure and its live output — which shows what the run said, the commands it ran and a failed call's first line, never a successful call's output — pass the denylist alone, which knows no value the engine handed the box: a vendor's key, a cloud session's access key ID and a Google access token, a JSON Web Token, a DigitalOcean token, a private key, a password, and an AWS session's secret access key and session token under the keys AWS writes them under. A credential of no shape it knows — an opaque key the box was handed, an AWS secret printed bare — is shown and recorded as printed.
- **A check's box carries nothing of a seat's, and never runs on the engine host as its user.** It runs a change's own code, so it holds no harness, no model's sign-in, no login and none of the operator's setup, only the secrets its check names, and `direct` is refused for it by name rather than warned about ([Check boxes](#check-boxes)).
- **A seat reads its own sessions whoever its turn acts for.** A session holds what the seat's runs read for whoever their turns acted for — a person's private room, a file only that person may read — and the seat's later turns read it back with `list_sessions` and `read_session` whoever they act for, as they read the seat's diary and its past turns: a transcript keeps no record of which entry needed whose permission, so no ceiling of who the reading turn acts for can be held over it ([A seat reads its own](#a-seat-reads-its-own)). Give a seat that reads one person's private work no work from anybody else, or keep no sessions (`turn_engine.conversation_session.enabled: false`), which withholds both tools from every model: no launch of that revision is offered them, and each call asks the revision its node runs, so a launch already running is refused at its next call once its node applies it.
- **Injected values are ephemeral.** `${VAR}` references are resolved at acquire time into that sandbox's process env only; the database stores references, never values, and the values die with the box.
- **Workers run on the engine host, never in a box.** A delegated worker — including one a run delegates to over its bridge — runs the engine's own tool loop, its harness's own tools denied and its grant cut from its parent's. No tool it can hold starts a run, and its control denylist keeps `delegate`, `a2a_ask` and the A2A channel tools, and the parent's discovery pair, from it by name.
- **Network egress is the provider's policy.** The engine does not enforce egress rules; use your E2B template's network policy for that, and prefer an allowlist (the LLM endpoint — the gateway its entry's `env` names, where it names one — the git host, the engine's tool bridge and OTLP receiver) in the templates your production agent seats' boxes run.
- **Budgets still bound the run** (previous section), the run's `submit_work` is decoded by the same submission rules as every phase's, and the turn passes the engine's delivery check before it reaches Review.

---

## Failure modes

### When a run does not start

An executor whose run couldn't be started fails its phase, and the turn with it: the run is the executor itself, so there's no other way to do the turn's work, and the turn's error says what failed. Each backend classes what it failed with at the point it fails, by **what would clear it**, so that whatever tells the failure to a model or a person can say that and nothing of the error itself, which names hosts, statuses, a container runtime's stderr or configuration paths:

| `why` | What failed | What clears it |
|---|---|---|
| `configuration` | The node runs no sandbox runtime for the company, or no tool bridge the run's box can dial (none at all, an `ingress` node on `api.port: 0`, or a cell whose box needs `CREWLET_MCP_BRIDGE_URL` to reach it, with that unset), a cell the catalogue doesn't have, an executor chain with no member whose harness runs in a box, a login that can't follow the run to its cell (`SandboxCredentialError`), a setup command that exited non-zero (other than 75), a create E2B refuses as malformed (400, 422) or a template it doesn't have (404), an E2B domain that doesn't resolve or whose certificate doesn't match it, an image the container runtime won't start (a pull that failed on a network blip included: only the runtime's own words tell the two apart, and Crewlet doesn't read them), a mount it can't share or the box may not write, a mount probe the runtime refused to run (see the lines below), or a box that can't run its shell, `find` or `head` to list a build | A person: an administrator fixes the sandbox setup. Waiting won't change it |
| `setup_unavailable` | A setup command that exited 75: its recipe saying that a download it makes is failing on the service's side and still was after its own retries (see [Setup steps](#setup-steps--provisioning-the-box)) | A wait, shortly |
| `provider_refused` | E2B refused the company's key (401, 403) | A person: an administrator fixes the provider's credentials |
| `provider_billing` | E2B wants the account paid for before it starts anything (402) | A person: an administrator checks the provider's account |
| `provider_limit` | E2B is at the account's limit of boxes or requests (429) | A wait: a box ending, or the limit's window passing |
| `provider_unavailable` | E2B or the box didn't answer, failed on its side (408, 5xx), or a box went away mid-command, or a container runtime didn't answer the mount probe in time, or a container would not start from an image the runtime does not hold (the image failed to arrive) | A wait, shortly |

The executor's phase and its turn carry the class as their `error_kind`, `sandbox_` before it (`sandbox_provider_refused`, …), so the one word an operator acts on is on the record beside the error. A launch this node cancelled (a drain, a lease it lost) is no class: it's `canceled`, however far the provider's request or the container runtime had got; one a person stopped is `stopped`, which is no failure.

A failure no backend classed isn't filed under the nearest class. A seat this node no longer holds, or one a person stopped, launches nothing, and the turn says so rather than that a launch broke (`executor_launch_stopped` for a stop). So does a seat that moves while its launch is under way, its box's setup included. What the launch then meets need not say so, since the seat's next holder ends a launching run without fencing it and the box's attach finds that ending, or no record at all; the turn's own fence is asked again whatever the launch answered, and a closed one hands the delivery on to the seat's holder (`turn_seat_moved`) rather than back for a retry here. A launch below the [budget floor](#budgets) is a budget refusal, and one whose every runner member is sitting out is a limited chain (both below); and a condition of the node (a coordination store that didn't answer, the launch running out of time) or a fault of the node itself carries no class at all. A box the engine host couldn't make a directory for is that fault.

What an operator should recognize:

- **`SandboxCredentialError` at launch** — the entry the launch picked places the seat's run in a **remote** cell, and nothing in the run environment signs in the harness of its [`providers.llm` entry](llm-backends.md). A login cannot follow it there: it stays on the engine host (it carries a refresh token whose rotation is fleet state), so unless something in the run environment authenticates, the harness would fail at its first model call with the vendor's own "not authenticated" — minutes in, naming nothing you could act on. Only a `claude-code` or `opencode` entry reaches this check: a launch passes over any other. The run never starts, and the error carries **your** remedy, chosen by the entry's `auth.mode` before its harness, because the mode decides what the entry reads at all. Under `api-key` it names the key: the variable `auth.api_key` references, else the harness's own key variable (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` for Claude Code), exported or stored with `crewlet secrets set`, or the seat's own in `role.sandbox.env` — never a headless token, which an `api-key` entry does not read. Under `inherit-env` it names the variables the mode forwards from the engine's own process environment, which the secret store is not part of: export one there, set one in `role.sandbox.env`, or run the entry in a local cell (`providers.llm.<key>.run_in: "direct"` or `"container"`, or `providers.sandbox.default_run_in` for an entry that names none). Both are worded as [`crewlet llm doctor`](llm-backends.md#how-doctor-judges-a-sign-in) words them, and an entry whose harness names no variable for its mode — `api-key` on `opencode`, which every write of the configuration refuses but an apply only warns about — is told to set `auth.mode: subscription` and run the entry in a local cell for the login it then signs in with. Under `subscription`, Claude Code, which mints a headless token, says "run `crewlet llm login <key> -capture-token`"; OpenCode, which reads its provider's key from its environment, names the provider its model names (`openrouter` for `openrouter/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5`) and says to set that provider's key in the entry's `env` or the seat's `role.sandbox.env`, both exported into the box — for OpenCode any credential-named variable in the run environment satisfies the check, since which variable it reads depends on that provider; and a profile that does neither — a `custom` one, or a built-in one whose `overrides` removed its token or its environment sign-in — says to run the entry in a local cell. A token or key variable the entry's `overrides` names outside the reference grammar (`token_env: MY-TOKEN`, which every write of the configuration refuses and an apply only warns about) is never offered under any mode: no secret store, `${VAR}`, export or token mint can carry a credential under it, so the refusal names that `overrides` field beside the routes the entry still has — OpenCode's provider key, or a local cell, under `subscription`; a forwarded variable in the grammar, or a local cell, under `inherit-env` — and alone under `api-key`, whose one variable it is.
  A credential you declared yourself in `role.sandbox.env` counts, and so does one the entry's own `env` carries into the box — for Claude Code a gateway's `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, or a Bedrock, Vertex AI or Foundry switch — since the check asks the harness's profile which variable names authenticate and looks for those in the merged run environment, rather than trying to recognise a credential by inspection. That is what lets it be a refusal instead of a warning. An unresolved `${VAR}` never counts: its key is [left out](#credentials-and-the-run-environment) of the run environment, so the check never sees it.
- **`this seat's executor chain is …, and no member of it can run the executor`**, from `crewlet validate` or a write — every entry the seat's `llm` names drives a harness with no in-box runner, so no launch could ever pick one and the seat would fail every turn. It is reported at the seat's `llm`, or, for seats that name none, once at the company fallback entry's `harness`, naming the first seat that falls to it. Point `llm` at an entry with `harness: claude-code` or `opencode` and keep the others for the reviewer, workers and side passes, or move one of them to one of those harnesses. It is a [runnable rule](configuration.md#what-a-stored-revision-is-held-to): refused on every write and by `crewlet validate`, and never applied: a node's reconcile refuses a revision that breaks it and keeps the epoch it was serving, and a boot, a reload or a revert refuses it by name.
- **`this seat has N tokens left and an executor run needs at least M (turn_engine.executor_min_budget_tokens)`** — the launch's [budget floor](#budgets). It is not a failure of the turn: on a dispatch the seat is parked until the window turns over or a revision moves a ceiling, logged as `seat_budget_parked` with `floor` and `starved` and a reason that names the window (`budget: <period> window <label> leaves N tokens, below the M an executor launch needs (turn_engine.executor_min_budget_tokens); resets …`), and a resumed segment is held on the same window. A budget that could not be read is refused too, and retried. Raise the ceiling, or lower the floor where the windows are right and the floor is not.
- **`seat "…"'s executor runs in a box, and this node has no sandbox runtime for this company: configure providers.sandbox`** — the launch found no sandbox runtime or no catalogue in the turn's revision. Validation refuses an agent seat with a model and no catalogue, so on a validated company this is a runtime that did not start on this node; its log says why.
- **`an executor needs a tool bridge its box can dial, …`** (or **`… a node serving its tool bridge, and nothing here reaches it`**) — the launch was refused because its box would have no address to reach the seat's tools at: a node with no bridge at all, an `ingress` node with `api.port: 0` that binds no listener for the bridge's route, or a `container` or `e2b` cell — or a `direct` one on a node that binds no listener its boxes reach on loopback (`api.port: 0`, or on a node without `ingress` an `api.host` of one address that is not loopback) — with no `CREWLET_MCP_BRIDGE_URL`. Set what the message names (see [the tool bridge](#the-tool-bridge--a-seats-own-tools-from-inside-a-box)): the refusal is classed `configuration`, which no wait clears. A node that runs seats says each such cell once when a company is applied, as `executor_bridge_unreachable`, before any turn is refused for it.
- **Every runner member sitting out** — every member of the seat's executor chain whose harness has a runner is sitting out a spent plan on this node, so the launch fails as a call whose chain ran out does, each member's refusal on it: a [limited chain](llm-backends.md#falling-back-between-entries). On a dispatch the turn fails (`llm_unavailable`, the seat's `last_error`, which is no stop) and the seat is parked on its provider (`seat_provider_parked`), reading `stopped` with the reason `provider` until a probe of the chain answers or the company's models change. A resumed segment's relaunch (a reviewer's send-back, a person's answer) that meets it parks the seat the same way, and its resume waits for the park's release: a completion held by the node that met the limit, in that node's memory and never on the run's record, until the instant the limit names, and refused at the admission while the park stands; an answer in its own series, uncharged (see [Both series are bounded](#mid-run-clarification-ask_and_wait)). A completion whose review's chain already sits out a limit parks the seat the same way once it is collected, before its turn runs. A run that ends on its plan's limit sits its entry out on the node that collects it (`harness_run_limit_reported`), so the turn's next launch moves on to the next member; a run that meets the limit midway is not relaunched on another member.
- **`sandbox_run_past_deadline`** (a log line, at info): a turn with a wall-clock cap — a scheduled fire's `timeout_seconds` — reached it with its run still working. The run is ended as if it had finished: its collection ends its job as a person's stop does (`sandbox_job_ended`, or `sandbox_job_killed` where it would not go), reads what it did up to then and marks it cut off, and the turn resumes with that. Raise the schedule's `timeout_seconds` if its runs routinely need longer.
- **`sandbox_deadline_end_unrecorded`** (a log line, at warning): a collection saw its job still working past its turn's wall-clock cap and could not record so on the run's record before it ended the job — the coordination store refused the write, and the error says why. That collection still says the cap cut the run off. One made again after it — the first cut short as it ended the job, or its box not read back — reads the job's own record of the request where its script left one; where the script left none, the run reads there as one that ended by itself, and is parked on a question it asked on its way out.
- **`sandbox_harness_end_unrecorded`** (a log line, at warning): a collection could not record on the run's record how its job's harness ended — the coordination store refused the write, or did not answer it within the room the read leaves its records, and the error says which. A relaunch that reads the record later and carries the job's round on in its box — a person's answer, the turn's kept session not what it continues — then reads the job as one whose harness may have been cut off, and tells the session there so, every call of the round and every note the turn has read, some of which it may hold ([A relaunch continues the turn's session](#a-relaunch-continues-the-turns-session)); its skill guard holds none of the job's own loads. A replaced sign-in carried on from that collection's own claim reads what the collection found.
- **`sandbox_prior_reclaimed`**, **`sandbox_reuse_failed`**, **`sandbox_prior_left_to_host`** (log lines): a relaunch did not continue in its turn's previous box — the member it picked runs another harness or in another cell, the box is seven days old or older (the line's `box_since`), the box could not be reattached, or the box is on another node's host — and started in a fresh one from the pushed branch (see *Reuse across launches* under [How a coding task runs](#how-a-coding-task-runs)). Nothing is lost but the box's disk state.
- **`executor_launch_stopped`** (a log line, at info): a person stopped the seat's running work while its executor was launching, so the launch was ended and the turn closes as stopped, naming who stopped it. Nothing needs doing.
- **`executor_session_fenced`** (a log line, at warning, naming the `step` — `upload` or `record` — and the `taker_epoch` the capture was taken under, followed by the capture's `executor_session_not_kept`): a keep of a run's session found that this node's [taker lease](#each-launchs-session-kept) no longer covers it — it lapsed and was claimed again at another epoch, or its renewals went unanswered past the window of the last one the store answered — so it uploaded and recorded nothing more, and stamped the capture refused, or unresolved where a record of it had already been sent and not answered. A peer may already have settled it from the executor log. Look at the coordination store's health on this node: one renewal the store does not answer costs nothing, and a keep is fenced only once two have gone unanswered in a row. **`executor_taker_lease_lost`** (at warning) is a lapse as the node's own renewal found it, after which the lease is claimed again at a new epoch; **`executor_taker_lease_unanswered`** (at warning) a claim or a renewal the store did not answer — while it holds no lease whose last renewal still covers it, the node begins no capture, and a collection's session is not kept; **`executor_taker_lease_refused`** (at warning) a claim the store answered and refused; **`executor_taker_lease_unreleased`** (at warning) a release at the node's stop the store did not answer within the stop's [one allowance](agent-runtime.md#graceful-shutdown), after which the lease lapses on its TTL. **`executor_session_settled`** (at info) is the other side: a node waiting on a capture whose taker's lease had lapsed read what came of it off the turn's row on the executor log, its `state` landed or refused — logged only where the settle was written, since one the taker's own stamp got to first leaves that stamp standing.
- **`setup step "…" writes …, which a direct box refuses`** (or **a container box**, or **an e2b box**), from `crewlet validate` or a write: a setup step writes a file outside the home of a local box some seat runs in, or one an `e2b` box refuses — another account's `~name/…`, or the home itself or a directory above it — so every box of that seat would fail at setup. The problem sits at the file's entry in the step, and names the first seat whose box it fails and how many more. Name the file relative to the box's home instead — `.npmrc`, `.local/bin/tool`, or `~/.npmrc` — which every cell writes under its home, or have a command of the step write it under `"$HOME"`: a `direct` box's home is minted per box, so no absolute path a file entry holds is inside it. Moving the seat to the other local cell helps only one way round: an absolute path under `/home/user`, which a `direct` box refuses, is taken by a `container` box, while a path outside the home is refused by both. Moving it to `e2b` helps for any absolute path the local cells refuse, `/home/user/…` and `/usr/local/bin/tool` alike, since that box writes both; it refuses another account's `~name/…` and the home itself or a directory above it, as both local cells do.
- **`local sandbox (run_in "direct") refuses to touch …`** or **`… is not a file under the sandbox home mount at /home/user`** (a `sandbox.LocalError`): a path a box was asked to write or read is outside its home, which neither local cell writes outside of — `direct` because the path would be the engine host itself, `container` because only the home is mounted. A setup file that names such a path is refused on the write (above), so at launch this is a stored revision that keeps such a step — an apply warns about it rather than refusing it — or a directory in the box's home that is a link leading out of it, which only the box can see: a link in a box's home is followed only while it stays there, so one the box's own work made must be relative and lead to somewhere inside the home. For a setup file the remedy is the one above: name it relative to the box's home, or have a setup command write it under `"$HOME"`.
- **`… neither docker nor podman is on the engine host's PATH`** (a `sandbox.LocalError`, when the company is applied or a node boots on it): `run_in: container` with no container runtime on the engine's own PATH, which under a service manager can be narrower than your shell's. Install one, put the directory that holds it on that PATH, or use `direct`. Naming one in `local.runtime` does not help: a runtime named outright is looked up on the same PATH.
- **`cannot see its own box directory`** — the runtime is driving a daemon on another host, so the bind mount is that machine's filesystem rather than this one's. Point `local.runtime` at a local daemon, or use `run_in: e2b` for a remote runtime.
- **`refused to run container sandbox …'s mount probe`**: the container runtime wouldn't run the probe in the box it had just started. The container exited at once (its image's own command failed), or the daemon refused the exec. The runtime's own words end the line. It's classed `configuration`, as every refusal of the runtime's is.
- **`did not answer its mount probe`**: the runtime didn't finish running the probe in the box it had started within 10 seconds: its daemon was too slow or wedged, or reading the mount hung. It's classed `provider_unavailable`, which a wait clears.
- **`cannot make a directory in its own home`**: the probe ran, and the container can't write its home mount, because it runs as a user the mount refuses. Run it as a user that can, with `run_args`, or mount the box directory writable.
- **`could not run its mount probe, because this host could not run the container runtime`**: the engine couldn't start the runtime's binary a moment after it started the container with it. That's a fault of this host, and carries no class.
- **`"container" needs the `local:` backend configured under providers.sandbox`** — an entry's `run_in` (or `default_run_in`) names a cell the catalogue does not hold. Add the block the cell needs, or point the entry at one that is configured; the message lists what this catalogue has.
- **`the catalogue offers more than one place to run one`** — an entry a seat's executor launches on names no cell and the catalogue names no `default_run_in`. Name the cell where the message points, or name the default. A catalogue is not refused for being ambiguous on its own — only a seat or entry that would actually fall to the missing default is.
- **`nothing names one, so no backend is built`** — an ambiguous catalogue that *nothing* reaches: no `default_run_in`, no entry's `run_in`. Nothing would be built and no seat could run code, so the block reads as configuration and configures nothing. Name the default, give an entry a seat's executor launches on a `run_in`, or remove the block while no agent seat runs on a model.
- **`writes its box directory as a user this engine cannot manage`** — the container is running as someone the engine cannot follow into the mount, which happens when `run_args` overrides the `--user` the backend chose. Drop that override, or run the engine as the same user the container does.
- **`sandbox_env_unresolved`** — a `${VAR}` in the run environment, or a setup file that is one whole `${VAR}`, resolves to nothing and its key is not declared optional; the executor run starts without it. The line names the keys and the `<step>:<path>` files, never a value or a body, and sorts the variables by why each is missing. It is at info when every variable is only `awaiting`, and a warning otherwise:
  - **`awaiting`**: an integration is setting the value up for the seat. A [provisioning pass](integration-reconcile.md) is minting it, and a run launched after its seal reaches this node is given it; or it is the seat's own `${GITHUB_APP_TOKEN_<HANDLE>}`, which nothing seals: the engine issues it at launch once the agent's GitHub App is created and installed (**Company › Integrations**), a run launched after the reconcile records the installation is given it, and a running job's token file is written by the first poll after the app can issue one.
