A token an integration issues at use also reads `awaiting` while the integration is still settling it (a grant its vendor is still applying): nobody has to act, the seat's tool servers ask for it again on the restart schedule until it is issued, and a running job's token file is written by the first poll after it is.
  - **`stopped`**: the integration that mints or issues it won't until a person acts. Act on its finding, which the line quotes; for the agent's GitHub App that is its **Create** or **Install** on the GitHub card.
  - **`unissued`**: the engine went to issue the agent's GitHub App token and GitHub didn't give one. Where GitHub didn't answer, or answered a 408, its rate limit (a 429, or a 403 its headers mark as one) or a 5xx, the line says GitHub is asked again on the restart schedule, and the first ask it answers issues the token. Where it refused, the line carries GitHub's status (a revoked key, a suspended installation, a permission the installation hasn't approved) and says the token isn't issued until somebody fixes the app: fix the agent's GitHub App, its installation or its permissions.
  - **`unset`** or **`empty`**: set the variable the line names (`crewlet secrets set`, or the integration's provision command for a seat's own bot or account), or list its key under `optional_keys` where the run can go without it.

  `optional_keys` never releases a variable an integration mints or issues for the seat: the line names it whatever the declaration says. And `${GITHUB_APP_TOKEN_<HANDLE>}` is the engine's to issue: it is never set with `crewlet secrets set`.
- **Setup-step failure** (`sandbox_setup_failed`) — a step's provisioning did not complete, and the box is torn down before any brief runs. The message names the step and says which of three things failed: `could not write <path>`, a file the box refused (in a local box, with the reason above), `command #N failed (exit S)`, a command that exited non-zero (in every cell, 124 is its `timeout_seconds` running out, with the timeout named before what the command printed, and a status above 128 is a signal that ended it — 128 plus the signal's number, 137 for a `SIGKILL` such as an out-of-memory kill — as a shell reports one; where the signal ended the box's own shell in a `direct` or `e2b` box, rather than a program that shell waited on, the signal is also named after what the command printed, while a `container` box's command reads as `docker exec` reports it, the status alone), or `command #N reported no exit status`, one the box could not start or whose box went away before it finished, with the box's own account after it — an `e2b` box's in-box agent closing the command's stream with an error of its own names that error there. A command that exited 75 is its recipe saying a download it makes failed for now, which a wait clears (`setup_unavailable`); any other failure is an operator config problem (a broken recipe, `configuration`). Either is logged distinctly from a harness install failure.
- **Sandbox lost mid-run** (`sandbox_run_lost`, a log line at warning, naming the `cause`): the job was lost with the host it ran on — `engine_restarted` (its engine's restart killed it), `box_gone` (its box is gone from where it ran) or `launch_node_gone` — and its round is carried on — continuing the lost job's kept session where it can, in a fresh run otherwise (`sandbox_run_lost_carried_on` on the node that holds the seat; see *A job lost with its host* under [Durability](#durability-completion-and-observability)). Nothing needs doing for one. A run whose turn then ends with `the executor's round was lost with its host too often to start again` lost more than two jobs in one round: it is taking its own host down, most often by exhausting the memory its container is held to — give the box more, or a cell of its own. Look for `sandbox_connect_failed` streaks before a `box_gone`; if the engine was down longer than `default_timeout_seconds`, E2B reaped the box as an orphan, and that TTL is the knob to raise for deployments with long maintenance windows.
- **Collect failure**: the box could not be read back. **It is asked again first**: nothing the completion writes — the charge, the run's phase record, the park, the resume — happens before a collection succeeds, so handing the claim back leaves the run exactly as the completion found it (running, its box kept alive, its turn parked), and the completion comes back on the queue's backoff while the poll fires a fresh one on its next tick (`sandbox_collect_retried`). The allowance is the poll's own for an unreachable box — at least two **consecutive** failed attempts spanning a minute, counted on the run's record so another node or a restart does not start a fresh one, and ended by a collection that did read the box (one whose park or resume then failed and handed the claim back), so a box that answered is never given up on the strength of failures from before it answered — and past it the box is torn down, the run ended and announced (`collect_unreachable`), and the agent freed. A box its provider says is **gone** is settled at once, since no attempt can read it. A collection **this node** stopped — a drain, a restart, the seat moving to another node — is handed back without being counted (`sandbox_collect_interrupted`), because it says nothing about the box; a collection that ran out of its delivery's time is counted, or a box whose read always outlasts it would be retried for ever. A file merely too large to read whole is never a collect failure: that piece is described by its size and the run is collected without it.
- **`sandbox_calls_drained`** (info), **`sandbox_calls_unfinished`** and **`sandbox_run_deleted_with_calls_in_flight`** (warning), on the node collecting a run: a collection waited for the calls its run's tool log held in flight before it read the log (`calls`, `unfinished`, `waited_s`) ([The tool bridge](#the-tool-bridge--a-seats-own-tools-from-inside-a-box)); it stopped waiting for one still in flight, read as a call whose outcome is unknown, naming it by its tool, its place and the first eight hex digits of its digest (`call`), the node process running it (`runner`) and why (`why`: its own bound passed, the turn's cap passed, or its process stopped) — what it committed is in no outcome of the turn unless its update lands while the run's record stands; or the run's ending deleted its record with a call still in flight there, whose update then finds no record.
- **`bridge_call_not_recorded`**, **`mcp_bridge_call_update_owed`** and **`mcp_bridge_call_update_unrecorded`** (log lines, at warning, on the node serving the run's tool bridge): the coordination store did not take a bridged call's write. `bridge_call_not_recorded` is a call's entry, written on the run's log before the call runs, which the store did not take within five seconds, four attempts included, or a log holding 256 calls in flight, or one that takes no more calls of the job (`why`): the call was not run, and its box was told so — that it could not be recorded and may be made again, or that its run has ended. `mcp_bridge_call_update_owed` is a finished call's update the store did not take within four attempts over 1.75 seconds, which outlast a stream leader's re-election — never a call one of them put in the log, whatever became of what it cost (written to the record after the log), since the attempts after that one try only that write: the call ran and its answer went back to the box, its entry stays on the log, so it is counted by its identity, and the update is asked for again every one to five seconds until the call's bound (`until`); `mcp_bridge_call_update_answered`, at info, says one asked again was answered. `mcp_bridge_call_update_unrecorded` is one still not taken at the call's bound: its entry stays, read as a call whose outcome is unknown, and what it answered, cost and committed is lost. A call that ends after a relaunch that carried the round on is listed in the round's log, marked `late`, and no warning names it, since it is on the run's record — the runs answer's `late`, and the Executors board's tag ([A relaunch continues the turn's session](#a-relaunch-continues-the-turns-session)). A call of an old round, which a new round replaced, is dropped, said only at debug, `bridge_call_after_its_job` — unless nobody waited for it, its job's session on the bridge having ended and answered it as still running, or its harness having given up on it: then neither the session that made it nor any record of the run holds what it did, though it ran, and it is named at warning, `bridge_abandoned_call_after_its_job`, by its tool, its place among the job's calls and the first eight hex digits of its digest (`call`). Each `bridge_` line names the run, the job and the call by its tool, its place among the job's calls and the first eight hex digits of its digest (`call`); each `mcp_bridge_` line names the run, the seat, the call's tool and its place (`seq`).
- **`sandbox_suspension_*` errors** (`executor_failed` with reason `suspension_unrecorded`): the turn parked but its parked state (`execstate`) never reached the run's record, because the runner recorded none, it would not serialize, it was past its share of the record however far the park condensed it (`sandbox_suspension_too_large`, which says whether the turn's own context or its closed rounds are past it), the coordination store refused the write, or the run was no longer launching. The run was already executing, so a run still launching has its box reclaimed at once and is ended, rather than left to run to its TTL with nothing able to collect it. A run that is no longer launching is left alone and not announced: a write reported as refused can have landed, which makes it an ordinary parked run the completion poll collects, and a run already ended was announced by whoever ended it. A store refusal points at the coordination store; a state past its share at the turn itself — a trigger whose own words are past it, or closed rounds a high `max_iterations` let grow past it; the other three are a defect in the engine worth reporting.
- **`executor_failed` with reason `claim_unreverted`**: the coordination store refused two writes on one row in a row. A completion claims a run's tail before collecting it, and every failure after that claim is recoverable only because the claim can be given back — so when the park a run asked for, or the revert a failed resume makes, cannot be written *and* the claim cannot be returned either, the run is ended here instead. The row would otherwise sit in `resumed`, which no completion poll reads, no redelivery can re-claim, no answer matches and no pause reaper expires: an unrecoverable turn plus a paused box billed until the seat changes hands. Look at the coordination store — this reason means it refused or could not be reached twice within one delivery.
- **`turn_rebased`** (a log line, not an announcement): a turn's unit of work began more than twenty-nine days before this attempt — a run resumed that long after its work began, or a trigger dispatched that long after it arrived — so its operation ids are minted at this attempt rather than at that start, and the instant is recorded so a later attempt at the same work inherits it (see [How a coding task runs](#how-a-coding-task-runs)). `seed` is the unit of work, or the run where there is none; `began` is the start, `inherited` the instant an earlier attempt had recorded (the start where none had) and `rebased_to` the new one. Nothing is wrong and nothing needs doing: it says that a tracker or knowledge-base write an earlier attempt made and this one repeats is a second write rather than a retry of the first (a work-item comment, a created item and a created view excepted, which are found by their ids and answered from their rows) — minted at the start, every data node's operation ledger would have answered every new write of this attempt `unknown` instead. It is logged once per rebase; an attempt that inherits a recorded instant logs nothing above debug.
- **`sandbox_answer_headroom_reserved`** (a log line, not an announcement): every message of this delivery has spent nearly all of its deliveries, so the answer route stopped offering it and the 5 that are left are kept for the ordinary route, which is being run on it now. It is what stops a reply being handed back until the broker dead-letters it, and it is the expected end of a resume that fails on *every* node rather than on this one — the per-process attempt count below cannot see that, because a seat handoff resets it and the broker's count is on the message. The line names the seat, what the partition had left and the reserve. A parked run is unaffected: it keeps waiting, bounded by `pause_ttl_seconds`.
- **`sandbox_answer_offered_over_a_spent_sibling`** (a log line): the opposite decision, and the one whose cost is paid elsewhere. A message of this delivery still has the deliveries to spare, so a parked run is being offered it — while another message in the same partition is inside the reserve, and a hand-back returns the whole partition. It is the advance notice for a `dead_lettered` line on that sibling, and it is the expected shape of a conversation whose earlier message has been handed back many times and which is still collecting fresh replies.
- **`sandbox_answer_resume_failed`** (a log line, at warning): the answer recorded on a parked run could not be resumed with — the error says why — and the coordinator will try again after the spacing the line names. The seat's later mail is held behind it meanwhile. Nothing is asked of the person: the answer is on the run.
- **`sandbox_answer_unverified`** (a log line, at error): the answer recorded on a parked run does not open as an answer to that run's question under the fleet keyring — the error says how — so it is handed to no turn, attributed to nobody and let go of with nothing handed back, and the run waits on its question again. A reply given after this answers it as usual.
- **`sandbox_answer_key_unknown`** (a log line, at warning): the answer recorded on a parked run is sealed under a key this node does not hold, and `node_holds` lists the ones it does. The answer stays recorded and owed, with the seat's mail behind it, until a node holding the key takes the seat or this one restarts with it in `secrets.keys`; the line repeats every five minutes while it waits.
- **`sandbox_answer_requeue_exhausted`** (a log line, not an announcement): this node could not resume one parked run with the answer recorded for it in every attempt its budget allowed — ten of them, spaced a second apart doubling to thirty, or as many as the run's `pause_ttl_seconds` left room for — so the answer was let go: the reply was handed to the seat's inbox as the ordinary message it is and is being worked as the turn it looks like, and the run waits on its question again with that reply declined for it. Its box is still bounded by `pause_ttl_seconds`. The line names the run and the window; the error beside it is the one to read, and it is nearly always this node rather than the run — no resumer on this node, a parked turn state of an `execstate` version this build does not read, or a seat that is not in this node's company. The seat's next holder, or this node after a restart, starts a clean set of attempts on the next reply.
- **`sandbox_answer_record_exhausted`** (a log line, not an announcement): this node could not RECORD one message as the answer a parked run is waiting on — the coordination store refused the write — in every attempt its budget allowed, so the message was let go to the ordinary route.
- **`sandbox_answer_lookup_exhausted`** (a log line, not an announcement): this node could not read *which* of a seat's runs is awaiting a reply — the coordination store refused the lookup — for every attempt the message's budget allowed, while the seat's own count said one of its runs is waiting for one. The message has been let go to the ordinary route and is being worked as the turn it looks like. The line names the seat and the delivery; the error beside it is the store's. `sandbox_answer_lookup_failed` is the same failure before the budget is spent, at warn, once per attempt.
- **`sandbox_claimed_settled_unread`** (a log line, not an announcement): the resumed turn came back (cleanly, or having broken after this attempt wrote outside the engine), and the run's record could not be read to find out which box to reclaim. The settle does not go quiet over that: leaving the row in `resumed` is the same unrecoverable state `claim_unreverted` exists to avoid. Instead it asks the coordination store to end the run **while it is still the claim it took**, which is what keeps it off a job the resumed turn relaunched: a relaunch moves the row back to `launching` and reuses the same box, so the ending declines it (`sandbox_claimed_settle_declined`) and that run is ended by its own turn or by the seat's next recovery pass. A store that refuses this too logs `sandbox_claimed_settle_kept`: the ending is kept, and this node finishes it, box included, once the store answers, or the seat's next recovery pass reaps the run if the seat moves first; both lines point at the coordination store.
- **`executor_failed` with reason `abandoned_tail`**: the node that held this seat stopped — a crash, a deploy, a lease that lapsed — between starting a run and writing the parked turn state it resumes from, or between claiming its tail and settling it, and the seat's new holder ended the run as it took the seat (`sandbox_abandoned_tail_reaped`). Nothing retries it: the turn that parked on the run cannot be continued. A claim for a person's answer that no turn had taken yet is not ended this way but revived and resumed with the answer (`sandbox_answer_revived`, below) — unless 3 claims of that answer have already died the same way, or the run's `pause_ttl_seconds` has passed since the first did: then the detail says which, and the answer has gone back to the seat as the ordinary message it is, once (`sandbox_answer_not_revived` names the bound with its `reason` and `lost_claims`; `sandbox_abandoned_tail_reaped` carries `answer_handed_back=true`, and the announcement's detail ends "the answer a person gave it goes back to the seat"). `answer_handed_back` is the field to search: the line's `answer_held` and `answer_taken` say only what the row held, and a held answer a turn took is held too. A line with `ending_decided=false` was written before the store confirmed the ending; its retry decides it, and the announcement it makes says whether the answer went back. An answer a turn had already taken was used by it and is not handed back (`answer_handed_back=false`). Look at why the previous holder stopped — and, past the revivals, at what stops a node between a resume's claim and its turn: three in a row is a resume that takes its node down, not a coincidence of deploys.
- **`sandbox_answer_revived`** (a log line, at info): the seat's new holder found a person's answer claimed for a resume by a node that stopped before its turn took it, and gave it back to the run, which it now resumes. `lost_claims` counts the claims of this answer that have died so far and `first_lost_at` dates the first; nothing is asked of the person. A `sandbox_answer_revive_owed` warning is the same holder unable to confirm the revival with the coordination store: it keeps the claim, holds the seat's mail and tries again every 30 seconds.
- **`executor_failed` with reason `resume_broken`**: the turn a run belongs to could not be re-entered — resuming it broke before the turn began, typically a panic decoding the parked turn state or re-entering the turn (`sandbox_resume_panicked` and a `turn.guard_breach` on the log). It is not retried, because every attempt re-enters the same bytes and reaches the same defect; a person's reply that drove the resume goes back to the seat as the ordinary message it is, and the detail says so. Look at the panic. A resume that broke *after* its turn began is not announced here: that turn published its own failed completion.
- **`executor_failed` with reason `state_retired`**: the run's parked turn was saved in a format no running build reads any more, because an upgrade retired it. A newer format waits for a node that reads it; a retired one is settled once no live node reads it (each node advertises the version it reads on its presence, `parked_state_version`, and a node that says none holds the settle back): when its seat is recovered (`sandbox_unresumable_run_ended`), or when an answer or a completion would resume it. Its question is withdrawn, a person's reply on it goes back to the seat once as the ordinary message it is, and the seat takes work again. Nothing is retried, so the answers a person gives such a run are never declined in a loop. The work it pushed, if any, is on its branch.
- **`executor_failed` with reason `item_finished`**: the run was parked on a question about a Worklet item that was finished or removed while it waited, so the question is moot. It is ended when the seat's holder next judges its parked runs (at the seat's recovery and on each work pickup pass) or when an answer would resume it; the question is withdrawn and a reply on it goes back to the seat once as the ordinary message it is. The work it pushed, if any, is on its branch.
- **`sandbox_handed_not_kept`** (a log line, at warning, on the node that launched the run): the job's box was attached and its job runs, but the secrets it was handed could not be written beside the run's record, so every [capture of its session](#each-launchs-session-kept) is refused rather than kept scrubbed of less than the box holds. The line names the run and its job, and the error says why — the coordination store unreachable, most often.
- **`executor_session_scrub_incomplete`** (a log line, at warning, on the node whose poll renewed a token in the run's box): the renewed token could not join what the job's box was handed — the set, its lapsed renewals dropped, would pass the 64 KiB a run keeps for its scrub, or this node could not open or seal it again (`reason` says which, and `error` the cause) — so the set is marked as holding less than the box, and every later [capture of the job's session](#each-launchs-session-kept) is refused saying so; the token is written into the box all the same. A later job of the turn in a fresh box starts a set of its own; one that continues in the same box keeps refusing, since that box still holds what the set could not. A set that keeps passing the bound is a launch handing its box close to 64 KiB by itself, most often a large file a setup step writes from a reference.
- **`sandbox_job_ended`** (a log line, at info, with `waited_seconds`): a stopped or lost run's job, or one its turn's cap passed while it still worked, was asked to end and was over within the wait ([Each launch's session, kept](#each-launchs-session-kept)); its session is taken as its script packed it, final. Nothing needs doing.
- **`sandbox_job_killed`** (a log line, with `why`): a stopped, lost or capped run's job was killed rather than waited for, and its session is taken after the kill, final where the kill ended it: `script_unheard` (at info) — its script records no end protocol, being one an older build started or one not yet at its first line; `not_the_jobs` (at info) — the pid its script recorded is no process of the job; `ask_failed` (at info, after `sandbox_job_end_unasked` at warning, which carries the error) — the box could not be asked, its runtime or its agent down; `outlived_its_end` (at warning, with `waited_seconds`) — the job was not over 2¼ minutes after its script was asked, a pack stuck or a call wedged; `read_cut_short` (at info, with `waited_seconds` and `error`) — the read itself ended first, its node draining or the node that asked for it no longer waiting, which says nothing of the job: it is killed all the same, its stop, loss or cap having decided its end, and the next read takes its session — a capped run's still saying the cap cut it off, read off the run's record. `outlived_its_end` is the one to look into: the box's error stream says what the script was doing.
- **`sandbox_job_unended`** (a log line, at warning): a stopped, lost or capped run's job was still running, or could not be read, after it was asked to end and killed — a `direct` box whose job's kernel record could not be read, so its signal was withheld (`local_sandbox_signal_withheld`), a container or E2B box whose kill could not run (`sandbox_job_kill_failed`, at warning, carries the error), or a container job its kill signalled nothing of (`sandbox_job_ungrouped`). Its session is taken as it stands, not final, and the box's reclaim ends the job — a capped run's once its turn's ending reclaims the box its collection paused.
- **`sandbox_job_ungrouped`** (a log line, at info, with `why`): the kill of a stopped, lost or capped run's job in a `container` box signalled nothing, because the box can name no process group of the job's own — its start recorded none (`local_sandbox_job_group_unrecorded`), being an older build's or on a runtime whose exec leads no session of its own, and its first process is gone or in a group no exec's own session leads. After a job's own end there is nothing more to it than strays its box's reclaim ends; with `sandbox_job_unended` after it, the job was still working and its session is taken not final. A runtime that logs it for every job runs its execs without a session of their own: runc gives every exec one.
- **`sandbox_collect_host_silent`** (a log line, at warning): the seat's holder asked the node whose host a run's box is on to read it back, and it did not answer — stopped, restarting, or gone. The claim is handed back uncounted and that node announces the completion again from its own poll once it is back; a node that never comes back has the run's job lost with it (`launch_node_gone`), and its round carried on.
- **`executor_failed` with reason `seat_removed`**: the seat left the company's agent seats — it was removed from the chart, or made a person's and not made an agent's again within the 24-hour retirement grace — so its runs were ended when its mailbox was retired. See [Seat Ownership § The removed seat](seat-ownership.md#the-removed-seat).
- **`steer_take_unanswered`** (a log line, at warning, with `notes`): a take of a run's notes was recorded by the coordination store while its answer was lost — a `steer_take` the store answered after its [five seconds](#notes-to-a-running-run), or a review's take whose segment ended before the store answered it — so the step that took them was never handed those notes. They are marked unanswered on the run's record: the turn carries them as read, and a launch that continues the run's session tells them again. Nothing needs doing for one; many of them say the coordination store answers slower than the hook's five seconds.
- **`steer_notes_unserved`** (a log line, at warning): a poll of an OpenCode run's notes could not record them [served](#notes-to-a-running-run) on the run's record — the coordination store did not answer — so it was answered without them, and they wait for the plugin's next poll, at least a second later. Nothing needs doing for one; a run of them is the coordination store's health to look at.
- **`steer_take_unsettled`** (a log line, at error): a take of a run's notes answered an error, and the coordination store has not answered the settle that marks it in thirty seconds of asking. The settle goes on asking until the store answers, and this line is said once for it; it is said again where the node stops first, once a last attempt at every take, made in the five seconds from the start of the stop's teardown (none for a take handed off once they are over), has not been answered either — and for a take lost once the stop has waited its settles out, which is not asked at all. Until the settle lands — and for good where the node stopped — the run's record says the run read notes its reader was never handed: a capture of its session counts them held, and a continuation of that session is not told them, though the review reads them. Look at the coordination store's health.
- **`steer_reader_never_ran`** (a log line, at warning, on the node that collects the run): a run whose harness takes a person's notes between its steps succeeded without asking for them once — a Claude Code run always asks at its finish, and an OpenCode run's plugin as soon as its session starts working — so something in its box switched its reading off: for Claude Code its [hooks](#claude-codes-hooks), by a managed policy that allows only managed hooks or disables them all, or a harness older than 2.1.163, which the run starts without them (its error stream says so); for OpenCode its [plugin](#opencodes-notes-plugin), by a managed policy that denies it or a load that failed, which OpenCode's own log names. Any note sent while it worked waited for its review rather than reaching it between its steps. The line names the run, its job and its harness.
- **`a hook in the run stopped its loop before it finished (Claude Code terminal_reason hook_stopped)`** — a hook of the repository's or the image's own settings exited 2 or returned a block, and Claude Code ended the run part-way while reporting a success subtype. The run is a failure saying so; the run's own steering hooks never block. Fix or remove that hook in the repository or the image.
- **`this box's home "…" cannot be named in an OpenCode run's configuration`** — an OpenCode run's configuration names its instruction file, its notes plugin and its servers' values by paths under the box's home ([OpenCode](#opencode)), and this home is one the configuration's text cannot carry: relative, or holding a brace, a quote, a backslash or a control character. A local box's home is under `providers.sandbox.local.state_dir`; move it to a path without one. The run starts nothing.
- **`crewlet: the run has no OpenCode configuration at …`** (the run's error output) — the configuration the runner writes before every OpenCode run was not there, or was empty, when the run's script started: something in the box removed it between the two. The script stops before it imports a login or starts a server, and the next run writes the file again.
- **A run that "finishes" with no report** — the harness neither wrote its findings file nor streamed a final message. `collect` surfaces the exit status, the harness's own account of how it ended (an error subtype, or that its stream stopped before its result) and the stderr as the error; the turn's collected record and its reviewer see a failed run rather than an empty success.

---

## Testing

`providers.sandbox.fake: true` wires the in-process fakes (`sandbox.FakeProvider`, `sandbox.FakeRunner`): scripted results, an in-memory filesystem, no network, per the project rule that tests never touch real services. A run's end on request is exercised against stand-in harnesses, under every `sh` a box image may have (dash, bash in its POSIX mode, busybox ash, and busybox's `kill` on any other): a harness told to stop, one that outlives its grace, a request before the harness starts, a second request during the pack, a pack killed midway, and an OpenCode run's interrupt through its own server; and each backend's end and kill against its own kind of box — a real `direct` box, a container runtime that runs each exec as its own session leader, as runc does, and an E2B agent's wire. The engine's tests script what a run does — its calls to the seat's tools over the real bridge, its `submit_work`, its question — and the end-to-end company runs its executor in a real `direct` box whose `claude` is the test binary. The runner tests pin the exact harness invocations (`claude -p … --output-format stream-json --verbose`, `opencode run … --format json`) and output parsers — Claude Code's against fixtures written from the vendor's documented stream-json message schema, and its hook lines and result messages against Claude Code's own output for 2.1.263 and 2.1.310 run against a stub model API (no model is ever called), and once through a real shell with a stand-in harness so the wrapper's copy of the result line and the harness's own exit status are exercised as they run; OpenCode's notes plugin runs, as the engine writes it into a real box, under `node` against a stand-in of the notes route, against a real bridge's own route, and against a stand-in of the context OpenCode hands a plugin — and `internal/config/examples_test.go` parses the git-auth recipe out of *this page* and holds it to its security properties (host scoping at both layers, `--add` rewrites, identity from the engine's agent facts) so an edit cannot quietly widen them. This is the copy readers take, so this is the copy under test.
